Password change keeps its own current-password-plus-code check and does not need a step-up.
- **Own sessions.** `GET /platform/auth/sessions` lists the caller's live sessions (`id`, `created_at`, `last_seen_at`, `ip_address`, truncated `user_agent`, `current`); `DELETE /platform/auth/sessions/{id}` signs one out. Another admin's session id, an unknown id and an already revoked id are all 404, so ids cannot be probed. Revoking the current session also clears the cookie. Audited as `session_revoked`. Token hashes are never returned.
- **Console.** A *My sessions* dialog (Sign out per row) and a step-up dialog. The API client treats 403 `step_up_required` generically: it opens the dialog, and on success retries the original request once, so no individual action is wired up. Cancelling shows the normal error. A 401 `session_idle_expired` returns the console to sign-in with an explanation.
- **Production validation.** `validate_for_production` rejects `PLATFORM_SESSION_IDLE_MINUTES` and `PLATFORM_STEP_UP_MINUTES` outside 1..1440. There is no setting that disables step-up or binding. Tests and e2e use helpers that really sign in and really step up, or age the stored timestamps in the database.
- **Migration** `d6f0b4c8e2a3` (after `c5e9a3b7d1f2`): `platform_sessions.ua_hash` and `step_up_at`. Idempotent and reversible.

**Consequences.** A cookie replayed from another User-Agent dies on first use and leaves an audit row; a stolen live session cannot mint new admins or codes without the authenticator; an unattended console signs itself out; an admin can end a session on a lost laptop. A user who legitimately changes browser or User-Agent string mid-session (a browser update, a "request desktop site" toggle) is signed out once and signs in again.

**Not done / residual risks.** An attacker with the cookie AND the same User-Agent (and IP when enabled) can still read everything and do non-sensitive writes (approve tenants, change plans) until idle or absolute expiry. Binding is not cryptographic (no DPoP or mTLS token binding). A session created minutes ago has a fresh step-up, so the five minute window after sign-in is not protected. Step-up is not applied to tenant status or subscription changes. An admin cannot list or end another admin's sessions (deactivation or a password reset does). The idle error code reveals to the cookie holder that the session existed. No notification is sent when a binding failure occurs beyond the audit row.

**Tested:** `backend/tests/test_platform_session_hardening.py` (User-Agent and IP binding on/off, idle expiry, `last_seen_at` throttle, step-up required / granted / expires / wrong code / replay / lockout / recovery code, own-only listing, revoke own / other, no token leak, production validation); Playwright `platform-session-hardening.spec.ts` (step-up dialog and retry, My sessions at 390 and 1280 px, idle message).


## ADR-084: Fernet key rotation (multi-key list and re-encryption script)

**Status:** Decided and implemented. Builds on ADR-045 (AI credential encryption) and ADR-074 (Platform Admin MFA secrets).

**Context.** One Fernet key (`AI_CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) encrypts Platform Admin TOTP secrets, tenant AI provider API keys and tenant SSO client secrets. There was no way to change it: swapping the value made every stored secret undecryptable, so a leaked or aged key could not be retired.

**Decision.**
- `AI_CREDENTIAL_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is a comma-separated list handled by one helper (`app/core/crypto.py`: `parse_keys`, `get_multifernet`, `encrypt_secret`, `decrypt_secret`) built on `cryptography.fernet.MultiFernet`: the first key encrypts, every key decrypts. All call sites already went through this module, so none changed. A single-key value behaves exactly as before.
- `validate_for_production` validates every entry; a malformed or empty entry refuses startup. Messages give the entry position only, never a key value, and the underlying exception is not chained.
- `backend/scripts/rotate_encryption_key.py` re-encrypts all three columns under the first key. Dry run by default (counts per column, including values already on the first key), `--apply` writes in one transaction, `--verify` checks every value decrypts with the first key alone. Idempotent (values already on the first key are not rewritten), refuses to start if any value is undecryptable (counts only), and prints no key, secret or ciphertext. Rows are locked `FOR UPDATE` and each update is conditional on the old value, so a concurrent change aborts and rolls back.
- RLS is untouched: the tenant-scoped `sso_configs` rows are read and written per tenant under the normal `app.current_tenant` setting, never with `session_replication_role` or a bypass role. The script uses `MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`.
- Runbook: Production Deployment Guide section 5a and Administrator Guide 1.6 (deploy `NEW,OLD`, dry run, apply, verify, deploy `NEW`, keep the old key while old backups exist).

**Consequences.** Rotation needs no downtime. A new encrypted column must be added to `TARGETS` in the script (a test enumerating columns named `*_encrypted` guards this). Losing all keys is still unrecoverable by design: admins re-enrol MFA, tenants re-enter AI and SSO secrets. No automatic schedule and no KMS/HSM integration.

**Tested:** `backend/tests/test_key_rotation.py`.


## ADR-085: Signed audit-chain checkpoints (GAP-01 partial mitigation)

**Status:** Decided and implemented. Builds on ADR-006 (tenant `audit_logs` hash chain), ADR-082 (`platform_audit_events`) and ADR-084 (key handling).

**Context.** Both audit chains detect an edit of a row, and the app role can only INSERT. But whoever holds database-owner rights can change an event and recompute every later hash, so the whole chain still verifies. Nothing outside the chain pinned its contents.

**Decision.**
- New table `audit_chain_checkpoints` (migration `e7a1c5d9b3f4`): `scope` (`tenant:<uuid>` or `platform`), the anchored row (`last_record_id`, `last_created_at`), `record_count` (rows up to and including it), `head_hash` (that row's `record_hash`), `prev_checkpoint_hash`, `checkpoint_hash`, `signature`, `created_at`. It holds ids, counts and hashes only, never audit content, so it has no RLS; tenant isolation is enforced in the API, which derives the scope from the session and never from input. No FK to `tenants`, so a checkpoint outlives its tenant (and `cleanup_dev_tenants` never has to touch it). The app role has SELECT + INSERT only (UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE revoked), like `audit_logs` and `platform_audit_events`.
- `checkpoint_hash` = SHA-256 over `scope|record_count|head_hash|prev_checkpoint_hash|created_at`, plus the anchored record's id and timestamp so those fields are covered as well (a small superset of the minimum). `signature` = HMAC-SHA256 of `checkpoint_hash` with `AUDIT_CHECKPOINT_HMAC_KEY`. Checkpoints of a scope chain to each other through `prev_checkpoint_hash` (genesis `0` x 64) and are written under a per-scope advisory lock.
- `audit_checkpoint_service.create_checkpoint(scope)` is idempotent: it returns nothing when the chain is empty or the head and count equal the last checkpoint's. It first runs the full verification and refuses (HTTP 409, nothing written) if the chain or an earlier checkpoint already fails, so tampering cannot be laundered into a fresh "good" anchor. Creating a checkpoint deliberately writes no audit event, otherwise every scheduled run would find "new activity" and checkpoint again forever.
- `verify_chain` (tenant and platform) keeps its walk, then always checks the checkpoints, even after a failed walk, and adds `checkpoints_checked` and `checkpoint_failure` (`null` or `{checkpoint_id, reason}`) to the result. `valid` is true only if the walk and every checkpoint pass; `broken_at_id` is still only about the walk. Reasons, checked in this order per checkpoint: `signature_invalid` (the stored hash does not match its fields, or the HMAC does not match), `checkpoint_chain_broken` (the previous-hash link is wrong, e.g. a checkpoint was removed), `anchor_missing`, `anchor_hash_mismatch` (the record at that id hashes differently or has another timestamp), `count_mismatch` (the number of rows up to the anchor differs).
- Writers: `POST /audit-logs/checkpoints` (tenant, `audit.view`, the same permission as verify and export), `POST /platform/audit-events/checkpoints` (platform session; no step-up because it only anchors what is already there), the CLI `python -m scripts.audit_checkpoint [--scope ...] [--dry-run]`, and the existing `worker` service, which runs every scope each `AUDIT_CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 3600, 0 disables) next to its SLA and report sweeps. Reads and export: `GET .../checkpoints` and `GET .../checkpoints/export` (a JSON attachment of hashes and counts, to be stored off-box).
- `AUDIT_CHECKPOINT_HMAC_KEY` is required in production: set, at least 32 characters, and different from the encryption key, SMTP and S3 secrets and any database URL (checked by `validate_for_production`, and a `${VAR:?}` guard in `docker-compose.prod.yml` for `api` and `worker`). In development an empty key falls back to a public, dev-only constant with a logged warning; production rejects that state.

**Where the protection actually comes from.** A checkpoint is only as strong as the secrecy of the HMAC key and the independence of wherever the checkpoints are kept. The key lives in the environment or a secret manager, not in the database, so a database-owner who rewrites `audit_logs` cannot forge a matching signature, and a rewritten chain no longer matches the anchored hash and count. Exporting checkpoints off the database host (a ticket, a SIEM, an object-lock bucket) is what lets you notice that the newest checkpoint(s) were deleted.

**Not done / residual risks (GAP-01 stays open, partially mitigated).**
- An attacker with database-owner rights AND the HMAC key can rewrite the chain and re-sign every checkpoint.
- Events written after the last checkpoint are not protected until the next one; the interval is the exposure window. Deleting only the newest checkpoint(s) is undetectable from inside the database unless an exported copy exists.
- No off-box anchoring is built in: nothing ships checkpoints automatically. The recommended next step is a scheduled export of `GET .../checkpoints/export` (or a worker job) to object storage with object-lock/WORM, or to an external timestamping or ledger service.
- No HMAC key rotation: replacing the key makes old checkpoints fail `signature_invalid`. Keep the old key to verify old checkpoints, or export first.
- Verification re-walks the whole chain and every checkpoint (linear in log size), as the chain walk already did.
- Tenant checkpoints are signed with one platform-wide key, not a per-tenant key.

**Tested:** `backend/tests/test_audit_checkpoints.py` (create and skip-if-unchanged, checkpoint chaining, HMAC, tenant isolation, permissions, app role insert-only, a consistent whole-chain rewrite via owner SQL that the plain walk misses but verification now fails, deleted tail events, count mismatch, tampered and forged checkpoint, removed checkpoint, refusal to anchor a broken chain, platform scope, CLI/worker runner dry-run and idempotency, production key validation); Playwright `audit-checkpoints.spec.ts` (create, verify, no-op create, export at 1280 and 390 px) and the existing platform Audit tab spec.

## ADR-086: User personal-data erasure (GAP-03, mechanism only, not a compliance claim)
**Status:** Decided. GAP-03 is **partially** resolved; see "Remaining gap".

**Scope of this ADR.** It provides a mechanism a tenant Admin can use to erase one user's personal data. It is not a statement that any tenant is GDPR/CCPA compliant: deciding whether and when to erase, and whether the retained data below is lawful to keep, is the tenant's (data controller's) call, ideally with its DPO.

**Correction to ADR-009.** ADR-009 said the audit hash chain is computed over a form that already excludes mutable PII, so pseudonymising would not break it. That is wrong for the current code: `record_audit_event` hashes the full `metadata` JSON, and several callers put an email or name in it (`user.created`, `user.invited`, `user.invite_accepted`, `user.scim_provisioned`, `user.sso_provisioned`, tenant bootstrap, `user.updated` with a new `name`). Those rows are hash-chained and anchored by signed checkpoints (ADR-006, ADR-085), so they must not be rewritten. This ADR therefore leaves `audit_logs` and `audit_chain_checkpoints` untouched. The earlier `POST /users/{id}/gdpr-erase` (no preview, no confirmation, did not revoke sessions or clean other tables) is removed and replaced.

**Mechanism.** `POST /users/{id}/erase`, permission `user.erase` (new, seeded to the system Admin role only by migration `f8b2d6a4c0e1`; an Admin could grant it to a custom role). Body `{dry_run, confirm_email}`; `dry_run` defaults to **true** and returns per-table counts of what would change and what is kept. A real run needs `dry_run=false` and `confirm_email` equal (case-insensitive) to the user's current email, and runs in one transaction. The user row is locked (`FOR UPDATE`) so concurrent erasures and the last-admin check cannot race. Rate-limited with `scaled(10)`. Cross-tenant ids are 404 (RLS plus explicit tenant filter).

**Refused (409):** erasing yourself; the last active Admin; a user already erased. Wrong or missing `confirm_email` is 422. Nothing changes on any refusal.

**Erased / changed** (marker: existing `users.pseudonymized_at`, exposed as `erased_at`; no schema change):
- `users`: name -> "Erased user", email -> `erased-<12 hex>@erased.invalid` (unique, non-routable), password hash replaced by the hash of a random unknown secret, `is_active=false`.
- `sessions`: all unrevoked sessions revoked (rows hold no personal data and stay). `mfa_challenges`: deleted.
- `notifications`: all addressed to the user deleted; the user's name in other users' notification titles replaced with "Erased user".
- `user_invites` for the user's email: email/name anonymised, token invalidated, expired.
- `report_schedules.recipient_emails`: the address is removed; a schedule left with no recipients is disabled, not deleted.
- An erased account cannot be reopened, renamed or have its password reset (409), including through SCIM.
- Audit: one new `user.erased` event with only the actor id, the user id and the change counts. No name, email or confirmation text.

**Kept (legal-basis call the tenant/DPO must confirm).** Business records are not blanked: comments (text included), defects, test cases, runs, execution results, requirements, project membership, background-job and idempotency rows keep pointing at the same `users.id`, which now resolves to "Erased user" everywhere a name is joined (lists, detail pages, audit actor names, exports). The reasoning is that these are the organisation's own work records and quality/audit evidence, kept under a legitimate-interest or legal-obligation basis, and that attributing them to a pseudonymous id is the proportionate step. Free text a user typed into those records (for example a comment that names them or contains their email) is not searched or edited. If a tenant decides that is not acceptable, that is out of scope here.

**There is no** avatar, phone, profile free-text, tenant-user MFA secret or per-user API token in the data model today, so there was nothing to erase for those (service and SCIM tokens belong to the tenant, not a user). Revisit if any is added.

**Remaining gap (GAP-03 stays open, narrowed).** Historical `audit_logs.metadata` rows written before erasure can still contain the user's email or name as typed at the time, and they cannot be changed without breaking the chain and checkpoints. The user's current name and email are gone from every live table, and audit lists show "Erased user" for the actor (joined by id), but a raw audit export contains those historical values. Fixing it properly means not writing PII into audit metadata from now on (store ids only), which stops new leakage but not old rows, or a documented crypto-shredding design (encrypt PII fields in metadata under a per-user key and destroy the key), which changes the hashed payload format and needs its own ADR. Neither is done here.

**Also not done:** erasure by request of the data subject is a manual Admin action (no self-service request workflow, no due-date tracking); backups and exported archives taken before the erasure still contain the data and age out under the tenant's retention; physical deletion of the `users` row is not offered (other rows reference it).

**Tested:** `backend/tests/test_user_erasure.py` (dry run is default and changes nothing, real run anonymises, sessions revoked and sign-in impossible, notifications removed, business records kept under the pseudonymous id, invites and report recipients cleaned, audit chain and checkpoints still verify and existing rows are unchanged, audit event has no PII, self / last admin / double erase / wrong email refused, Manager and Tester get 403, permission held by Admin only, cross-tenant 404) and `frontend/e2e/user-erasure.spec.ts`.

## ADR-087: Audit hash-chain load test, streaming verification, and `since_checkpoint` (GAP-12)
**Status:** Decided. GAP-12 is narrowed with measurements, not closed for production sizing.

**What was measured.** `backend/scripts/audit_chain_loadtest.py` (not part of pytest; creates and drops its own database, never touches the dev database, refuses `ENVIRONMENT=production`). Run on a slow, memory-starved Windows laptop with Postgres in Docker on the same machine, Python writers as threads in one process. Figures are indicative of this machine only and varied by about 1.5x between runs, because the database, the test harness and the browser tooling share the same CPU and disk.

- **Write load**, 20 writers, 2,000 events, one session and one transaction per event through `record_audit_event`: one tenant 69-105 events/s (p50 184-278 ms, p95 273-385 ms, max 306-444 ms, of which almost all is waiting for the advisory lock); two tenants with the same 20 writers 109-173 events/s (p50 114-176 ms). 0 errors in every run. After each run an independent check found no fork, no gap and strictly increasing timestamps, and `verify_chain` returned valid.
- **Tenants do not share a lock.** The key is `hashtext(tenant_id)`, so two tenants take different locks, and a second tenant raised aggregate throughput by about 1.6x. It was not 2x because commits (WAL flush), the single Postgres instance and the Python process are shared. A `hashtext` collision between two tenants would make them share a lock (harmless, only slower); it was not observed.
- **A single tenant is serial by design**: about 10-14 ms per event here (lock held across read-head, insert and commit), so roughly 70-100 events/s per tenant is the ceiling on this machine. Latency under 20 concurrent writers to one tenant is therefore queueing, not work.
- **Verification cost** (before / after the streaming change): 10k rows 0.8 s / 1.2 s, 50k rows 4.6 s / 6.0 s, 100k rows 17.5 s / 16.4 s; peak Python memory 25 / 126 / 253 MB before, a flat 6 MB after. Time grows linearly (it is CPU bound on re-hashing in Python); four checkpoints added no measurable cost at these sizes (their count queries are indexed by tenant; timings differ only by machine noise).

**Change.** `verify_chain` (tenant and platform) now reads rows through a server-side cursor in batches of 2,000 (`yield_per`) selecting plain columns instead of loading every ORM entity, so memory no longer grows with the log. The hash and link rules, the legacy no-`service_token_id` acceptance, the single-statement snapshot and the response are unchanged. When the chain is broken, `checked_count` is now the number of rows walked up to and including the first bad row (before, it was the whole log size); no client reads it as a total.

**`since_checkpoint` (tenant verify only).** `GET /audit-logs/verify-chain?since_checkpoint=true`. When all signed checkpoints verify and at least one exists, the walk starts after the newest checkpoint's anchor row, whose hash is trusted through the HMAC signature and which was already position-checked by checkpoint verification. The response gains `skipped_count` (default 0) with the number of records not re-hashed; `checked_count` is rows walked. Default behaviour is the unchanged full walk. If any checkpoint fails, or none exists, it falls back to the full walk. **Trade-off:** an edit inside the checkpointed prefix that changes a row's content without moving the anchor is caught by the full walk but not by this mode (it is tested and documented as such). Use it for frequent checks; run the full walk periodically.

**Still unproven (see GAP-12).** One machine, one Postgres, threads in one process (not several API replicas or a networked database); writes of about 100 bytes of metadata; no sustained multi-hour soak; sizes above 100k rows per tenant were not built (extrapolation from linear growth: roughly 3 minutes at 1M rows on this machine, not measured); checkpoint count queries were measured with 4 checkpoints, not an hourly series over years; platform-chain verification shares the code shape but was not separately timed; the verify request still holds one HTTP worker for its whole duration (no job offload, no timeout). The export is still capped at 50,000 rows (ADR-039).

## ADR-088: Malformed ids are a 422 with the standard envelope (GAP-89 follow-up)

**Context.** GAP-89 describes a one-off `psycopg` UUID error that was never reproduced. Sweeping every route with a malformed id (`backend/tests/test_malformed_ids.py`) did reproduce a different, real 500: `require_resource_permission` and the defect-closed policy rule read `request.path_params` directly and ran `UUID(raw)`; dependencies resolve before FastAPI reports path-validation errors, so `GET /test-cases/not-a-uuid` raised `ValueError` and returned `internal_error` (500).

**Decision.** (1) Those two dependencies treat an unparseable id as unresolvable and let the route's typed path parameter answer 422. (2) A `RequestValidationError` handler returns 422 in the `{data, error}` envelope (code `validation_error`, message from the first error) instead of FastAPI's `{detail: [...]}`, which the frontend previously rendered as a generic "Request failed". (3) A `DataError` handler returns an enveloped 422 (`invalid_value`) without echoing the driver message, as a safety net. 422 (not 404) is kept because existing routes already use 422 for malformed typed ids; unknown well-formed ids remain 404.

**Trade-offs.** The 422 body shape changed for all validation errors (no code in the repo read `detail`). The sweep covers tenant routes with UUID-typed path/query params; body ids are covered by one representative test (they are Pydantic-typed). Platform routes are not swept. The original GAP-89 concurrency symptom remains unreproduced.

## ADR-089: In-app documents are served by the API behind a session (issue 32, GAP-95)

**Context.** The `/brd` Documentation viewer (ADR-072) bundled copies of 19 documents into the front-end build (`import.meta.glob` with `?raw`, one lazy chunk each). The route needs a session, but the text did not: anyone who could fetch the static assets (the dev server serves `/src/content/docs/database-schema.md` directly; a production build ships the chunks as plain `.js`) could read the Administrator Guide, database schema, API inventory and the rest without signing in. The viewer's own comment admitted the internal set was "a relevance filter, not an access control".

**Decision.** (1) New router `GET /api/v1/docs` (the documents the caller may open, plus display groups) and `GET /api/v1/docs/{slug}` (metadata plus Markdown), both requiring a signed-in tenant user, rate-limited with `scaled()` and answering `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. (2) A registry in `app/services/docs_service.py` classifies each document: general (user guide, product documents, requirement traceability, the architecture diagrams) readable by any signed-in user; sensitive (Administrator Guide, Production Deployment Guide, Security Configuration, Backup and Restore, Known Gaps, Architecture, Architecture Decisions, SaaS Architecture, Database Schema, API Specification, Test Strategy, Design System) need Admin. Sensitive documents are not listed for others, return 403 on a direct request, and the viewer shows the "No access" screen for a deep link. An unknown slug is 404; a slug that does not match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$` (traversal attempts included) is 422 before any lookup, and the slug is only ever matched against the registry, never joined into a path. (3) "Admin" is the permission `tenant.settings.manage`, the same test the web app's `isAdmin()` and the `/settings` route gate use, so no new permission or migration was needed and custom roles that hold it are treated the same in the UI and the API. (4) One source of truth stays in `docs/`; `python -m scripts.sync_docs` (also `npm run docs:sync`) copies the registered files into `backend/app/docs_content/`, which the api image carries because its build context is `./backend`; `--check` (and a backend test, skipped where `docs/` is absent) fails on drift or orphans. (5) The front-end bundle holds no document text; `frontend/src/content/docs` and the old sync script are gone, Mermaid stays lazy-loaded.

**Trade-offs.** Managers lose access to the engineering set (ADR-072 gave it to Admin and Manager); the two new audiences are "any signed-in user" and Admin. Sensitive documents added to the registry (ADRs, Known Gaps, deployment/security/backup guides) were not in the viewer before. Editing a document now needs `docs:sync` and an api image rebuild to show up, not a web rebuild. Documents are still readable by every tenant's users (they describe the product, not tenant data); they are not secret from an Admin, who can copy them. The text is no longer stored in the browser's disk cache (`no-store`), so each open is one request. The registry-to-docs drift is detected by test, not by CI wiring beyond the existing backend suite.

## ADR-090: Accessibility gate policy: every impact level, explicit allowlist (issue 34, GAP-22)

**Context.** The Phase 10.9 accessibility checks failed only on `serious`/`critical` axe findings and used WCAG 2.0 tags, so minor/moderate findings and the WCAG 2.1 and best-practice rules were never enforced.

**Decision.** (1) `e2e/a11y.ts` defines the one scan every new accessibility spec uses: axe with `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa` and `best-practice`, reporting every impact level. (2) `e2e/a11y-sweep.spec.ts` (signed-in routes as Admin, Manager and Tester, and the public pages) and `e2e/mfa-a11y.spec.ts` (sign-in/MFA screens and Platform Admin dialogs) fail on ANY finding not listed in the spec's `ALLOW` map; an entry needs a rule id, a reason and a matching row in KNOWN_GAPS. The map starts empty because the first run's findings were fixed at their shared source (table header, export button, landmarks, headings). (3) New screens must pass this gate; the older serious-only specs stay as a cheaper smoke.

**Trade-offs.** The gate is stricter than a conformance claim needs (best-practice rules are advisory in WCAG) and will block a change for a minor finding; the allowlist is the pressure valve. It is still automated-only: manual keyboard and screen-reader review remain open (GAP-22, GAP-96). Animated sections can report transient colour contrast, so scans of animated pages wait for the animation to settle.


## ADR-091: Public home page performance: no auth wait, smaller first chunk, cheaper layout (issue 40)

**Context.** The public home page (`/`) scored Lighthouse mobile 51 with a 5.9 s LCP. Two facts shaped the work. (1) The compose `web` service runs the Vite dev server, which is not representative: all measurements here were taken against the production image (`frontend/Dockerfile.prod`, nginx), run beside the stack. (2) On `/`, `HomeGate` waited for `/auth/me` before deciding between the Dashboard and the marketing page, so a signed-out visitor saw "Loading…" until the round trip finished, then still had to fetch the marketing chunk. The hero text, images (`width`/`height`, `fetchpriority` on the eager one) and fonts (`font-display: swap`, one preloaded Inter file) were already sound.

**Measured (one Lighthouse 13 mobile run each, simulated throttling, slow memory-starved machine, so absolute numbers are pessimistic and noisy; repeat runs of the same build varied by several points and by seconds of TBT).** Before: score 64 (second run 64), JS transfer 230 KB, total 419 KB, Speed Index 6.2-7.2 s, TBT 2.7-4.8 s. After: score 65, JS transfer 147 KB, total 331 KB, Speed Index 5.8 s, CLS 0.001 in both. Lighthouse's headline LCP (about 1.1-1.4 s) is the "Loading…" / first paint in both builds; the lab's own LCP breakdown reports the real hero paint 3.3-4.2 s after navigation, essentially render delay (main-thread work), which was not materially reduced. TBT did not improve in the lab after the change (it was higher, mostly unattributed main-thread time) and we could not establish why; treat the lab TBT as unresolved, not as a win.

**Decision.** (1) `HomeGate` renders the marketing page immediately when `localStorage` has no `qtesthub:session-hint` (set by `AuthProvider` once `/auth/me` succeeds, cleared when it fails); a signed-in browser keeps the old wait, so there is no public flash. The hint is a rendering hint only, never an access decision; a user upgrading from an older build sees one transient public render. (2) Login, Signup, AcceptInvite and AppShell are lazy routes, and every marketing page except Home is lazy, shrinking the first chunk (main entry 166 KB to 76 KB transferred, gzip). The reveal-on-scroll observer re-scans when a lazy page commits. (3) `content-visibility: auto` on `.lp-section` skips style/layout for off-screen sections (Style and Layout halved in the lab). (4) `nginx.conf`: `gzip_vary`, `gzip_comp_level 6`, `gzip_min_length`, more compressible types, and `Cache-Control: no-cache` on `index.html` (hashed assets stay `immutable` for 30 days).

**Not done / trade-offs.** No Brotli (stock nginx lacks the module; terminate Brotli at the edge proxy). No image re-encoding (dashboard.jpg is 110 KB; Lighthouse estimates 87 KB savings from modern formats). The remaining cost is main-thread rendering of the full home page DOM and the hydration of the app entry (react-query, router); deferring below-fold sections' mounting is the next candidate. Field data (RUM) is the only trustworthy confirmation. `content-visibility` makes the scrollbar length estimate until sections render.


## ADR-092: RBAC Phase 4: time and environment access rules (release freeze) (issue 43)

**Context.** Phases 1-3 gave project-scoped roles (ADR on `project_members.role_id`), a ctx-based policy registry (`app/core/policy.py`, ADR-058 area) and resource-keyed permission checks (`require_resource_permission`, `resource_not_locked`). Phase 4 of that plan, time and environment rules such as a release freeze, was not built. Test runs carry no environment field in this codebase, so an environment rule cannot apply to them; only defects carry an environment (free text or an Environment row).

**Decision.** (1) New tenant table `access_rules` (RLS like every tenant table, migration `a9c3e7b1d5f2`): name, enabled, `rule_type` (`freeze_window` | `environment_restriction`), optional `project_id` scope (NULL = whole tenant), optional `environment`, `starts_at`/`ends_at` (timestamptz, UTC), `blocked_actions`, `exempt_roles`, `reason`, created_by/at. Off by default: no rows, no behaviour change; the permission `access_rule.manage` is seeded to Admin only. (2) Enforcement reuses the existing mechanism: a new ctx rule `access_rules` in `_CTX_POLICY_RULES`, invoked by `require_permission`, `require_project_permission` and `require_resource_permission` after the permission check, for mutating methods only (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS return before any database access). The rule is matched on the permission code the route already declares; `blocked_actions` entries are an exact code or a dotted prefix (`testrun` matches `testrun.create`, `testrun.finish`), validated on create against the real permission table. It loads the tenant's enabled rules with one indexed query (`ix_access_rules_tenant_enabled`), and resolves the action's project from the path (`project_id`, `test_case_id`, `defect_id`, `run_id`, `test_run_case_id`) only when a project-scoped rule exists. A hit raises `403 blocked_by_access_rule` whose message is the rule's admin-written reason, with no other rule detail. (3) `freeze_window` blocks inside `[starts_at, ends_at)`. `environment_restriction` blocks actions on a named environment (case-insensitive) for non-exempt roles, always, or outside an optional allowed window; it is evaluated in `defect_service` for defect creation and the set-environment endpoint, both as `defect.create`. (4) Admin is always exempt (it is forced into `exempt_roles`, and `access_rule.manage` can never be blocked), so a rule can always be lifted; Manager/Tester exemption is configurable. A custom role is evaluated by its mapped tier (manager or tester), consistent with the rest of the code. (5) The clock is the server's UTC time; the UI shows local time with an explicit timezone label and the UTC time. `evaluate()` in `app/core/access_rules.py` is a pure function with an injected clock. (6) API: Admin-only CRUD at `/access-rules` plus `POST /access-rules/preview` (dry run); `GET /access-rules/active` is open to any signed-in user and returns only the active rules restricting their own role (reason, end time) for the UI banner. All writes are audited as `access_rule.created|updated|enabled|disabled|deleted` with name, type and scope only (no PII, the reason text is not copied into the audit log). (7) UI: Admin screen at `/admin/access-rules` (gated by `access_rule.manage` in `routeAccess.ts`) and a banner in the app shell for freeze windows.

**Not done / trade-offs.** One-off windows only: no recurring weekly schedule. No per-user rules (roles only). No named action groups beyond permission-code prefixes (the form offers testcase, testrun, executionstep, defect, requirement). Routes guarded only by `get_current_context` with the service layer doing the check (for example defect status/root-cause/build-release edits, `PATCH /defects/{id}/environment`'s permission check) are not covered by the route-level hook because they declare no permission code; the environment restriction is the exception, applied in the service. Project scoping is not resolved for routes whose path has none of the ids above (for example `execution-steps/{id}/verdict`); a project-scoped rule does not apply there, a tenant-wide rule does. There is one rules query per mutating request even for a tenant with no rules (indexed, no caching, so a change is effective at once). The banner shows only freeze windows; a banner on a test run page for a project-scoped rule is not shown because the run page URL carries no project id.

## ADR-093: Tester visibility switches: assigned cases only, hide Reports (issue 44)

**Context.** Two BRD items: Testers should be able to be limited to the test cases they are responsible for, and Reports should be withdrawable from Testers. Both must be per tenant and must change nothing for any tenant that does not opt in.

**Decision.** (1) Two boolean columns on `tenants` (`testers_see_assigned_cases_only`, `reports_hidden_from_testers`, both `false`; migration `b1d4f7a2c9e3`), exposed through the existing `GET/PATCH /settings` (Admin, `tenant.settings.manage`) and, effective for the caller, on `GET /auth/me` as `tester_case_scope` and `reports_hidden` (always false for Manager/Admin) so the UI needs no extra request. Every flip writes one `tenant.setting_changed` audit event holding only the key and the new boolean. (2) One central module, `app/services/tester_visibility.py`, owns the filter. A case is "assigned" to a Tester when `test_cases.assignee_id` is them, OR a `test_run_cases.assigned_to` for the case is them, OR the case is in a non-deleted run whose `executed_by` is them. Only the tester access tier is affected (`role_code == "tester"`, which includes custom roles mapped to the tester tier, ADR-059); a project-role override granting `project.view.all` exempts that project. (3) Wired through that module: both case lists (so totals and paging), case detail/history/versions/compare (404, not 403, so existence is not leaked), attachments and comments on a case (via `owner_access`), test data sets list, global search, repository tree module counts, requirement detail linked cases, the CSV/XLSX case export, the AI assistant context, and the dashboard test-case counts, domain distribution and the test-case aggregate metrics. Writes keep their existing permissions (an unseen case is 404 for any by-id case operation). (4) Reports: with the switch on, every `/reports/*` endpoint returns 403 for a Tester via one dependency (`require_reports_not_hidden`); `/dashboard/*` stays open. The Dashboard hides the two report-backed widgets (status chart, execution trend) and keeps summary, needs-attention and activity. The sidebar, command palette and route gate hide `/reporting*` through `canAccessPath`. (5) A guard test enumerates the GET test-case routes so a new one cannot skip review.

**Not done / trade-offs.** Requirement coverage counts and `linked_test_case_count` still count all links (titles are filtered, counts are not). Defect screens can still show the title of the case a defect is linked to. Tester project access (assigned projects only) is unchanged. A Tester's own run created from cases they later lose sight of keeps them visible (run owner rule).

## ADR-094: Execution Console cockpit mode (issue 45)

**Context.** Testers run long sessions in the Execution Console and asked for a darker, denser, bigger-target layout without changing the app theme for everyone.

**Decision.** Frontend only. An opt-in toggle (`aria-pressed`, "Cockpit mode") adds a `.cockpit` class to the console wrapper; a dedicated dark token set scoped to that class (own surfaces, not an automatic flip of the theme) restyles the existing components. The preference is stored per browser in `localStorage` (`qtesthub:cockpit`, guarded; default off). Layout: verdict buttons at least 56px (48px phones), sticky header with progress, step timer and blocked state, reduced-motion respected. The tokens pass WCAG AA (see `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`) and `@media print` forces the light tokens. No backend, schema or API change.

**Not done / trade-offs.** Dialogs (block, defect) are portalled outside the wrapper and keep the app theme. No keyboard shortcut was added, to avoid clashing with P/F/I. The preference is per browser, not per user account.

## ADR-095: Landing page navigation, flow and motion (issue 46)

**Context.** The public home page had a sticky header, reveal-on-scroll and a mobile menu, but no in-page orientation (no progress, no section links, no way back up), a header that did not condense, a menu without a focus trap or scroll lock, and hash links that did not move keyboard focus.

**Decision.** Frontend only, no dependency, copy and design tokens unchanged. (1) Header: now `position: fixed` over a constant 64px spacer, so condensing (shadow and a 56px bar after 24px of scroll) never moves content. Nav links get an animated underline. (2) Mobile menu: `role="dialog"` named "Site menu", Tab trapped between the toggle and the menu, body scroll locked while open, Escape closes and returns focus to the toggle, 160ms close animation, 44px targets; navigating remounts the header, which closes it. (3) New `PageChrome.tsx`: a scroll progress bar (a `scaleX` transform written from a rAF-throttled scroll listener, no React renders), a back-to-top button after one screen (lifted above the phone demo bar), and on the home page a desktop-only (>=1280px) dot rail built from `homeSections.ts` with labels on hover/focus and `aria-current="location"` set by an IntersectionObserver. The footer repeats the same section links. (4) Hash navigation (`MarketingSite`) now also focuses the target (adding `tabindex=-1` if needed) after scrolling; `scroll-margin-top` is 80px. (5) Hero: a "See why teams switch" cue links to the next section; only the metric row has an entrance animation. The h1, lead and buttons (the LCP) are never animated. Existing reveal observer, stagger delays, `CountUp` and the sticky phone CTA were reused. (6) Buttons and cards press in on `:active`.

**Reduced-motion policy.** Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` every animation and transition is off, reveals are never hidden (the observer does not arm), the hero entrance, nudge and press transforms are disabled, scrolling is instant, and the menu closes immediately.

**Performance guardrails.** CSS-first; only `transform` and `opacity` animate (header height transitions on an out-of-flow element); no layout-affecting changes on scroll; `content-visibility` on `.lp-section`, lazy marketing pages and `HomeGate` are untouched; the chrome adds about 3 KB to the marketing chunk.

**Not done / trade-offs.** The pricing teaser is a home section without its own link in the header (the header links stay page level). The rail is hidden below 1280px, where the progress bar and back-to-top remain. Active-section detection samples headings, so a very long section keeps its dot until the next heading arrives.

**Follow-up (same issue).** The rail now has a compact form below 1280px: a "Sections" pill (bottom-left, 44px targets, Escape closes, `aria-current` kept) that opens a short list. The active section is the last heading above a reference line at 35% of the viewport (rAF-throttled), so long sections keep their dot and scrolling up updates it. Quality Intelligence is now on the home page after Roles (and in the rail and footer). Bottom-fixed UI shares one CSS variable, `--lp-fixed-bottom`, set while the phone demo bar is visible; back-to-top and the pill sit above it, so a future banner only adds to it. The spacer moved to `MarketingSite`, so the header markup is not re-indented.

## ADR-096: Rerun failed only, version diff polish and saved views (batch 1)

**Context.** Testers re-create runs by hand after fixes, the version compare screen was a flat list of lines coloured by change, and every list page forgot a filter combination on the next visit.

**Decision.**
1. *Rerun failed only.* `POST /test-runs/{id}/rerun-failed` creates a new run holding only the `failed` and `blocked` run cases (never passed, ignored, pending or in progress) of an open or finished run. It copies the type, per-case assignee and data row, names the run `<name> (rerun)`, stores `test_runs.original_run_id` (new nullable self FK) and writes audit event `testrun.rerun_created`. 409 when nothing qualifies. It uses the run-creation permission `testrun.create` through the resource-aware dependency (project role overrides and RBAC Phase 4 access rules apply, so a release freeze blocks it with its reason) plus the same project-access check as creating a run, so strict assignment (ADR-059) holds: a Tester can only rerun in a project they belong to, and copied assignees keep their assignment. Each case is pinned to its current version when still approved, otherwise to the version the original run executed. Test runs have no build, release or environment columns today, so none are copied.
2. *Version diff.* Frontend only; the compare payload (title, description, per-step old/new action and expected) was sufficient. `lib/versionDiff.ts` does a word-level LCS diff and the summary line; steps show an icon plus a text label (Added, Removed, Changed) and highlighted words are `<ins>`/`<del>` with hidden "added:" / "removed:" text, so change is never colour only. Priority and other case fields are not in the compare payload and are not diffed.
3. *Saved views.* Table `saved_views` (RLS, `user_id` owner, `list_key` in test_cases, defects, test_runs, name up to 60 characters, `filters` JSONB, `sort`, `is_default`). Views are private to the owner: another user's id is 404 and there is deliberately no sharing, so no permission code is needed. Filters are validated against a per-list allowlist of keys with typed string values (uuid, date, token, short text), at most 2KB; unknown keys are rejected rather than stored, so a view is never arbitrary JSON. Unique `(user_id, list_key, name)`, a partial unique index allows one default per user and list (setting a default clears the previous one), at most 25 views per user per list (409). The list pages apply the default only on first open when the URL has no filters.

**Not done / trade-offs.** No sharing, no team defaults, no audit events for views. The existing browser-local "Saved filters" menu on Defects and the Test Repository is unchanged next to the new Views menu. The Test Repository has no Views menu.

## ADR-097: Defect leakage report and manual flaky-test quarantine (batch 2)

**Context.** Managers could not see how many defects reach production or a shipped release, and a flaky test either stayed in every run or had to be deprecated.

**Decision.**
1. *Defect leakage and test effectiveness.* `GET /reports/defect-leakage` (and `/export.xlsx`), filterable by `project_id`, `date_from` and `date_to` (defect report date, inclusive). **Definitions.** A defect has *escaped* when (a) it is linked to a release through `fixed_in_release_id` and that release has shipped, or (b) its environment is a production environment. Releases have no status column, so *shipped* means `release_date` is set and is today or earlier (a release with no date, or a future date, is not shipped). Environments have no production flag, so *production* is a name rule: the Environment name (or, for older rows, the free-text environment) equals `production` or `prod`, case-insensitive; no flag was added. *Leakage %* = escaped / total, *detection effectiveness %* = (total - escaped) / total, both `null` when there are no defects (shown as "No defects", never 0%). Open escaped defects (status New or Open) are bucketed by age (under 7 days, 7 to 30, over 30) and escaped defects are also split by severity. The per-release table groups by linked release; defects with no release form a "No release" row (they can still be escaped through the environment rule). A monthly trend feeds the chart and its table alternative. No migration. Visibility reuses the Reports rules: a Tester sees only projects they own or belong to (403 for another project) and gets 403 on both routes when the tenant hides Reports from Testers (ADR-093).
2. *Quarantine.* New `test_cases` columns `quarantined` (default false), `quarantine_reason`, `quarantined_by`, `quarantined_at` (migration `d7f3b9a1c5e2`). Quarantine is manual only. `POST /test-cases/{id}/quarantine {reason 3-500 chars}` and `POST /test-cases/{id}/unquarantine` require the existing `testcase.approve` permission (Manager and Admin; Testers get 403) through the resource-aware dependency, so project role overrides and access rules apply; a scoped Tester would get 404 for an unseen case. 409 when already (or not) quarantined. Audit events `testcase.quarantined` (metadata holds only `reason_length`) and `testcase.unquarantined`. The case list filter `quarantined=any|only|exclude` (default any) is also on the all-projects list and in the saved-view allowlist (`quarantined`). Run creation (`POST /projects/{id}/test-runs`, `POST /test-suites/{id}/create-run`, `POST /test-runs/{id}/cases`) accepts `exclude_quarantined` (default false, so current behaviour is unchanged); with it set quarantined cases are skipped and a request that would leave no case is a 422; the run audit event records `excluded_quarantined`. Rerun-failed and verdicts on a case already in a run are unaffected. `release-readiness` gains `quarantined_count`, the leakage report gains `quarantined_test_cases`, and flaky-test rows gain `quarantined`, so quarantined cases are always counted separately and never silently as passing. The flaky report shows a Quarantine action to users with the permission, and cases and the case page show a Quarantined badge (icon and text).

**Not done / trade-offs.** No automatic quarantine, no expiry or review reminder, no per-run override. Releases linked only through a test plan or run are not counted (defects link to releases only through `fixed_in_release_id`). Quarantined cases are not excluded from existing runs or from readiness percentages, only reported beside them.

## ADR-098: Saved report builder

**Context.** Managers kept asking for one-off lists ("open critical defects by project") that none of the fixed reports covered.

**Decision.** A structured, allowlist-driven builder, never SQL or expressions.
1. *Definition.* JSON `{source, fields[], filters[], group_by[], sort}` validated by Pydantic with `extra=forbid`; every field, operator and value is checked against a fixed per-source allowlist (`app/schemas/report_builder.py`), otherwise 422. Only the server maps allowlisted names to SQLAlchemy expressions (`report_builder_service.py`); nothing from a definition reaches SQL text. Limits: 12 fields, 8 filters, 2 group-by, 20 values per `in`, values up to 200 characters, definition JSON up to 8KB, page size up to 100, exports up to 5000 rows, 50 saved reports per user, name up to 80 characters. Each query runs with `set_config('statement_timeout', '10000', true)` (10s, transaction local); a timeout returns 422 asking for narrower filters.
2. *Sources and fields.* test_cases: id, title, priority, status, module, assignee, quarantined, created_at, project. test_runs: id, name, run_type, status (derived: open or finished), executor, created_at, finished_at, project, total_cases, passed_cases, failed_cases. defects: id, title, severity, status, priority, environment, reporter, assignee, created_at, project, release (fixed-in). Operators by type: text eq/neq/in/contains/is_null; enum eq/neq/in/is_null; id eq/in; number eq/neq/in/gt/gte/lt/lte/is_null; date gt/gte/lt/lte/is_null; bool eq. `contains` is case-insensitive with `%`, `_` and backslash escaped. Aggregates are count and count by group only. Person fields show a display name only; an erased user is always "Erased user". No email, hash, token or MFA column exists in any source.
3. *Visibility.* Every run reuses the Reports rules: tenant RLS plus an explicit tenant filter, project scope (Tester: owned or member projects; Admin and Manager: whole tenant), the Tester case visibility setting for test_cases (`tester_visibility.scope_case_query`), and `require_reports_not_hidden` (403 for Testers when Reports are hidden) on every route. A shared report runs with the runner's visibility, not the owner's.
4. *Storage and sharing.* Table `saved_reports` (RLS, migration `e8a4c2f6b9d1`), unique (owner, name). `is_shared` makes it visible, runnable and exportable (read only) to every user who may use Reports; only the owner or an Admin can edit or delete (others get 403 on a shared one, 404 on a private one). Permission is the existing `report.view`; no new code. Audit events `report.saved`, `report.deleted`, `report.exported` carry name and source only.
5. *Export.* CSV (UTF-8 with BOM) and XLSX of the saved definition, max 5000 rows; text cells starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR are prefixed with a single quote (shared `safe_cell`).

**Not included.** Scheduling a saved report by email (the schedules table is limited to the compliance report and the worker renders it without a per-user visibility context, so it is not a cheap extension); drag and drop ordering (fields are ordered with arrow buttons); cross-source joins; sum and average; test case type and updated-at fields (not stored on test cases today).

## ADR-099: Custom dashboards and widgets

**Status:** Accepted. **Context:** `/` (Dashboard.tsx) is one fixed layout. Users want their own boards of the charts and tables the product already has, without a layout engine or new dependencies.

**Decisions**
1. *Fixed layout.* A dashboard is a named, ordered list of widgets on a 3-column grid. Size S, M, L spans 1, 2, 3 columns; every size stacks to one column on phones. Ordering uses move up / move down buttons and a size select. No drag and drop, no new dependency.
2. *Privacy.* A dashboard belongs to one user (`owner_user_id`). Anyone else gets 404, as if it did not exist. Limits: 10 dashboards per user, 12 widgets per dashboard, name 1-60 characters, settings at most 1 KB.
3. *Team default.* An Admin (`tenant.settings.manage`) can publish one of their dashboards as the tenant team default: it is stored as a copy with no owner (`is_team_default`), replaces the previous team default, and a unique partial index allows one per tenant. Every user lists and can open it read only; only an Admin can edit, delete or unset it. Users with no dashboards of their own see it under "Team default" on `/dashboards`.
4. *No new data queries.* The API only stores layouts. Each widget reads its data from the existing endpoints with the viewer's own session, so every visibility rule (project scope, Tester case scope, Reports hidden from Testers) applies unchanged. The frontend keys widget data per user (`['dashboards','widget', userId, ...]`).
5. *Typed settings.* Per widget type a Pydantic model with `extra="forbid"` validates settings on save (unknown keys and types are 422). A `project_id` must be a project the saver may see. It is re-checked on every read: if the viewer can no longer see it, the widget is returned with `project_access: "denied"` and shows "No access to this project" without calling its endpoint. A `saved_report` widget's `report_id` must be visible to the saver on save; a later 404 shows the inline error.
6. *Atomic save.* `PUT /dashboards/{id}/widgets` validates the whole list first and replaces it in one transaction; a failure keeps the old layout.
7. *Reports hidden.* Widgets that read `/reports/*` (execution trend, test case status, defect aging, release readiness, defect leakage, flaky tests, saved report) show a note instead of loading for a Tester when `reports_hidden_from_testers` is on. Summary tiles, recent activity, open defects and my assigned test cases use endpoints that stay open.
8. *Audit.* `dashboard.created`, `dashboard.deleted`, `dashboard.team_default_set` (set or unset), metadata is the name (plus `enabled` for the team default).

**Catalogue (11 types).** kpi_summary, execution_trend (days 7/14/30/90), status_distribution, defect_aging, open_defects_by_severity (summary severity counts plus defect status counts), release_readiness (project required, release optional), defect_leakage, flaky_tests (project required, top N 1-50), recent_activity, saved_report (report id, first 10 rows), my_assigned_cases (the existing test case listings filtered by assignee). Every widget except saved_report takes an optional `project_id` (required where the endpoint is per project). Charts have the usual "View as table" alternative.

**Storage.** `dashboards` and `dashboard_widgets` (RLS, migration `f9b3d5a7c1e2`; widgets cascade with their dashboard).

**Not included.** Drag and drop; a free-form or resizable grid; sharing an individual dashboard with chosen users; widget-level cross filters; a per-user choice of home page (`/` stays the default Dashboard).

**Follow-up:** the separate "Dashboards" sidebar entry was removed. The home Overview (`/`) and custom dashboards share one "Dashboard" entry through a View switcher; `/dashboards` remains as the "Manage" page, reached from the switcher.

## ADR-100: Colour themes, Appearance picker and custom palette

**Decision.** Besides System, Light and Dark (unchanged), users can pick three preset themes (Cobalt & Sunshine, Sage & Tangerine, Indigo & Aqua) or build up to three custom themes. The choice is stored per browser, not per account.

**Mechanism.**
1. *One derivation.* `frontend/src/lib/themeDerive.ts` (pure, no imports) turns base mode + primary + accent + surface tint into the full token set: surfaces, `--border`, `--border-strong`/`--control-border` (3:1), foregrounds, `--primary`, `--accent` (+ foregrounds), `--link`, `--ring`, status colours (same hues as the defaults, so a status reads the same everywhere and always keeps its icon and label), chart chrome, tactile shadows and the sidebar. The presets are the same function run on `PRESET_INPUTS`; the output is committed as `:root[data-theme="cobalt|sage|indigo"]` blocks in `index.css` (static CSS, so no flash and no script needed). A unit test fails if a block drifts from the function.
2. *Contrast policy.* Foreground on a fill is white or a tinted near-black, whichever reads better (yellow and aqua never carry white). Primary is moved darker (light base) or lighter (dark base) until it is 3:1 against every surface; link, muted text and the sidebar text are moved until 4.5:1 against every surface they can sit on; the ring and control border reach 3:1. Each adjustment is reported to the user ("Primary was darkened ...") and the editor shows an "AA pass" badge with the lowest ratio and a details list. `accent` is a fill only (never text, never a sole indicator).
3. *Application.* `<html data-theme>` is `light`, `dark`, `cobalt`, `sage`, `indigo` or `custom`. Custom sets the derived tokens as inline custom properties on `<html>` plus `color-scheme`. `public/theme-init.js` runs before first paint on every page (app, login, signup, platform admin, marketing, docs): it maps the stored id to `data-theme` and, for Custom, validates and injects the tokens cached under `qtesthub:theme-custom` (names `--[a-z0-9-]`, values `#rrggbb` or `rgba(...)` only). `useThemePreference()` still returns the resolved light/dark scheme (charts, Mermaid); `useThemeChoice()` returns the full choice.
4. *Storage.* `qtesthub:theme` (system = key removed), `qtesthub:custom-themes` (`{v:1, activeId, themes[<=3]}`: id, name <= 24 chars, base, `#rrggbb` primary/accent, tint), `qtesthub:theme-custom` (derived cache). Everything read back is validated strictly and invalid entries are dropped; every access is in try/catch and the theme still applies for the page view when storage is blocked.
5. *Unchanged.* Cockpit mode keeps its own token set (and `--control-border: var(--border)`); print stays light.
6. *Dialog polish.* Shared `Dialog`/`DialogFooter`: 24px desktop / 16px phone padding, 44px close button, sticky footer with 44px actions (stacked full width on phones, primary last), token backdrop (`--overlay-backdrop`) with blur, bottom sheet with handle on phones. Menus use 40px items and raised-surface panels.

**Contrast (WCAG ratios, all asserted by `src/test/themes.test.ts`; worst surface):**

| Theme | Body | Muted | Primary btn | Accent btn | Link | Primary / ring | Control border |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cobalt & Sunshine | 17.8 | 5.08 | 8.44 | 12.33 | 6.82 | 7.17 | 3.03 |
| Sage & Tangerine | 14.99 | 5.99 | 7.93 | 7.25 | 4.88 | 5.67 | 3.02 |
| Indigo & Aqua | 17.88 | 5.21 | 7.84 | 8.88 | 6.33 | 6.37 | 3.06 |

Light and Dark are unchanged (new tokens `--accent`, `--accent-foreground`, `--border-strong` only; their soft `--border` is not held to 3:1). 200 random plus extreme inputs (#ffff00, #000000, #ffffff ...) all reach AA through the derivation.

**Not included.** Per-account sync of the choice across devices; theme export/import; an exit animation for dialogs (entrance only, none under reduced motion); a high-contrast theme.

## ADR-101: Landing page colour scheme (Cobalt & Sunshine) and landing-page audit

**Decision.** On public marketing routes (`/` for signed-out visitors, `/platform`, `/features`, `/solutions`, `/quality-intelligence`, `/pricing`, `/about`, `/contact`, `/request-demo`, `/security`, `/privacy`, `/terms`) a visitor with no explicit saved theme (nothing stored, or System) gets the Cobalt & Sunshine preset (Cloud surfaces, Cobalt primary, Sunshine accent, dark text on yellow, never white). Any explicit saved choice (Light, Dark, Sage, Indigo, Custom) wins. `public/theme-init.js` applies it before first paint (no flash); `MarketingSite` re-asserts it and hands `<html>` back to the app theme (`refreshTheme`) on leaving. The signed-in app and `/login` etc. keep the OS rule.

**Why.** Cobalt/Sunshine is the preset with the strongest accent-on-dark and CTA contrast (ADR-100) and gives one unmistakable action colour; making it the site default, not a forced theme, keeps the saved choice authoritative.

**Implementation.** `marketing.css` keeps its `--lp-*` variables but they now map onto semantic tokens (`--background`, `--muted`, `--foreground`, `--primary`, `--accent`, `--sidebar-*` for the dark bands, `--status-*`), so all five themes work. Dark bands (nav, hero, Quality Intelligence, differentiators, CTA panel) are theme-aware via the sidebar tokens and never adjacent. Hero/CTA on dark bands use `--accent` with `--accent-foreground`; light surfaces use `--primary`. Translucent white overlays and neutral shadows on always-dark bands stay literal. Contrast of 21 landing pairs in all five themes is asserted by `src/test/marketingContrast.test.ts`.

**No fabricated social proof (rule).** No invented logos, testimonials, ratings or metrics; only claims true of the product and verifiable in this repo. FAQ additions (data protection, data region) state facts from docs (data region is readiness metadata only, no per-region hosting).

**Audit** (pass / fixed / missing):

| Rule | Result |
|---|---|
| Plain headline, subheadline names audience | fixed (lead now names QA, engineering and release teams) |
| Hero visual is a real product screenshot | pass (existing TOUR screenshots) |
| One primary CTA + quiet secondary | fixed (3 buttons -> 1 CTA, microcopy says what happens, tour as text link) |
| Outcome headings, hierarchy, spacing | pass (no copy rewrite) |
| CTA repeated mid/bottom | pass (CtaPanel, sticky mobile CTA) |
| 65ch body, >=16px | pass (lead 16-18px, max-w on leads) |
| Social proof | missing (none exists; gap left, nothing invented) |
| FAQ / objections | fixed (+ security, data region) |
| Privacy / terms / contact in footer | fixed (links added; `/privacy`, `/terms` are placeholders marked pending legal review) |
| Forms: labels above, required/optional, errors | pass (8 fields in demo form; trimming is a backlog item) |
| Nav minimal, ADR-095 behaviour | pass (unchanged) |
| 44px targets, 48px primary CTA | fixed (hero/CTA min-h-12; legal links 44px) |
| AA contrast, five themes | fixed (token mapping + test) |
| Alt text, image sizes, ADR-091 LCP guardrails, reduced motion | pass (untouched) |
| No horizontal scroll 390/768/1280 | pass at 390/1280 (asserted); 768 not run |

**Still missing.** Real social proof; real legal text (needs counsel); Lighthouse re-run vs issue 40 (score 65, CLS 0.001) not run; indigo/light axe on `/` and a11y-sweep not run (contrast covered by vitest).

**Follow-up.** The section-rail dot is ringed with `--background` then `--foreground` so it reads on light and dark bands in every theme; the demo form requires only name, work email and company (role, team size, requirement and message are optional, matching the backend schema), with required/optional marked and inline errors.
A visitor with the `qtesthub:session-hint` key on `/` gets the app default instead of cobalt (theme-init.js and MarketingSite); marketing-only paths keep cobalt.

## ADR-102: Project dashboard as the default project tab

**Status.** Accepted. **Phase 1.**

**Decision.** `/projects/:projectId` opens a read-only Dashboard tab. A tab strip (Dashboard, Details, Requirements, Test Cases, Test Runs, Defects) links the existing project routes; the previous project page (details, members, recent lists, run status chart) moved to `/projects/:projectId/details`. Breadcrumb reads Projects > project name > Dashboard or Details.

**Widgets.** The existing ADR-099 widget components with this project's id preset, in a fixed layout under three headings: Health (summary, release readiness for the project's latest release or the whole project, test case status), Trends (execution trend, defect leakage), Risk & activity (open defects by severity, defect aging, flaky tests, recent activity). Loading, error and Retry behaviour, the hidden-Reports note for Testers and project access are inherited from the widgets; a non-member opening the project gets the same load-error state as before.

**Not in scope.** No customization, no new tables, no backend change. Quick actions (new run, report defect) were skipped.

**Deep links.** Every in-app link to `/projects/:id` (project cards, run and case lists, defect detail) now lands on the Dashboard tab; bookmarks to the old page land there too. The old content is one tab click away.

## ADR-103: Button hierarchy, labels, sizes, states and password inputs

**Status.** Accepted. **Phase A.**

**Rules.** One primary (default variant) action per screen or dialog; others are outline or ghost. Destructive actions use the destructive variant and are never the only primary. Labels say what happens ("Create dashboard", "Save layout", "Confirm identity"), not Submit/OK/Yes/Save. Phones: the default variant is 48px tall (`max-md:min-h-12`), every other button and the password toggle 44px minimum; desktop stays compact.

**States.** `Button` has hover, a token-based pressed state (tactile emboss via `--elevation-pressed-sm` on all variants, so all five themes inherit it), the focus-visible ring, disabled, and `loading` (spinner, `aria-busy`, disabled, label and width kept). `ConfirmDialog` uses `loading` instead of swapping its label; wired on the sign-in, sign-up, accept-invite, dashboard and step-up submit buttons.

**Inputs.** `PasswordInput` adds a show/hide toggle (`aria-pressed`, label "Show characters", input name/autocomplete untouched). `PasswordStrengthMeter` (text plus bar, `role=status`, pure `passwordStrength()` in `lib/passwordStrength.ts`) appears on the sign-up, accept-invite and password reset/change flows only. `FormField` already wires `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby` and `role=alert`; the sign-in email field now does too.

**Skipped.** Quantity steppers (no quantity field exists), tag input and date masks (no field needs them). The one-time-code field stays a single input. Only the most-used submit buttons got `loading`; the rest still use `disabled`.

## ADR-104: Interactive cards, phone tab bar behaviour and the desktop icon rail

**Status:** accepted.

**Primary-button audit.** One primary (default-variant) button per visible group, found by scanning every route and component for default-variant `<Button>`. Only one real conflict: Dashboard editing (`DashboardView`) showed "Save layout" and "Add widget" together, so "Add widget" is now outline. Everything else already had one primary per toolbar or dialog footer (list pages: the create action; dialogs: the confirm action; destructive actions use the destructive variant). Per-card inline "Add" buttons on Project detail and Settings were left as the primary of their own card form.

**Cards.** Only clickable summary cards (dashboard KPI tiles via `EmbossedCard interactive`, class `.qth-card-interactive`): 24px padding (16px under 640px), 8px inner gap, 1px token border, token shadow, hover lift 4px over 200ms ease with the shadow stepping up (hover devices only), pressed state settles back, focus ring on the stretched link, all motion removed under `prefers-reduced-motion`. A tile is one primary link (stretched `::after`) plus at most one secondary link above it; no nested anchors. Inner radius is `max(0, panel radius - padding)` (`--card-inner-radius`). Data tables, forms and dense lists are untouched; the ProjectsList cards are phone renderings of the data table, so they were not changed.

**Phone tab bar.** A transform-based sliding indicator (200ms, instant under reduced motion) over five equal columns. Badges: Defects shows open defects (New + Open from the cached `/defects/status-counts`), More shows unread notifications (same cache key as the bell); both hidden at 0, and the badge text is `aria-hidden` with an sr-only suffix (accessible name "Defects, 1 open"). Hide-on-scroll (`lib/scrollDirection.ts`, pure and unit tested): hides after scrolling down more than 48px in one run, returns on any upward scroll or at the top. It never hides on the Execution Console (`/test-run-cases/*`, pinned verdict bar), while the More sheet or any modal dialog is open, while keyboard focus is inside the bar, or under reduced motion.

**Desktop icon rail.** The collapsed sidebar (64px, persisted in localStorage, tooltip on hover and keyboard focus, active accent bar) gained thin dividers in place of section headings. Expanded state unchanged.

**Skipped.** Two-level sidebar, floating sidebar, scroll-direction logic on desktop, card treatment for list/table rows.

## ADR-105: Shared table preferences, row density and Test Cases bulk actions

**Status:** accepted.

**Shared prefs.** `useTablePrefs(tableKey, columns)` (alias of `useColumnPrefs`, `components/table/tablePrefs.ts`) is the one hook for visible columns, resized widths and row density. It persists to localStorage per user and per table (`qtesthub:table-prefs:<table>:u:<userId>`, falling back to the older user-less key; storage failures are swallowed). `ColumnVisibilityMenu` is the one control: a "Row density" Comfortable/Compact toggle plus the column checkboxes. Compact only tightens desktop rows (`md:` cell padding via `data-density` on the table, about 32px of padding plus content); phones render cards and ignore it, so tap targets stay 44px. Title/name and status columns are `alwaysVisible`; defaults are unchanged. Applied to Test Cases, Test Runs (project list and cross-project overview), Requirements, Defects, Test Repository and the Traceability Matrix.

**Bulk actions (Test Cases).** Row checkboxes (header "Select all on this page", per row "Select <title>"), at most 100 selected, kept across pages while the filters are unchanged and cleared when they change. A sticky bar (fixed above the tab bar on phones, `aria-live` count) offers Assign, Change priority (both via the ADR-070 `/test-cases/bulk-update`), Move to module and Quarantine/Unquarantine (per-item endpoints, 3 in parallel, reason required to quarantine). Actions follow the user's permissions (`testcase.edit`; `testcase.approve` for quarantine, ADR-097); the server stays authoritative, so strict assignment (ADR-059), access rules and Tester scope surface as per-item failures. The toast reads "N updated, M failed (reasons)"; failed rows stay selected for retry. No backend change.

**No bulk delete.** Deleting is irreversible and a stray select-all makes it too easy; deletion stays a deliberate per-case action.

## ADR-106: Quick-create test case, requirement-link combobox, Create and start

**Status:** accepted.

**Already existed.** Full case form (Drawer), step rows, shared-step insert, dirty-form guard, `createTestCase`, requirement<->case link/unlink endpoints (`POST/DELETE /requirements/{id}/test-cases`), New Test Run dialog with "Skip quarantined cases (N)", DefectQuickCreateDialog. **Missing and added:** a compact create path, a case-side requirement control, a one-click run start.

**Quick add.** `QuickCreateTestCaseDialog` (Test Cases header "Quick add"; the project dashboard's "New test case" opens it via `?quick=1`): Title, Priority, Module, 1-3 steps (Enter in Expected adds the next row, Ctrl/Cmd+Enter creates). Same endpoint and rules as the full form (title >= 5 chars, at least one complete step, because the API requires one). Inline errors use `aria-invalid` + `role=alert`; server reasons (access rules, strict assignment) are shown verbatim; closing with entries asks first. "Create and add another" keeps priority and module, clears title and steps, refocuses Title. "More options" opens the full form with the entered values (`lib/quickCase.ts`, pure and unit-tested).

**Requirement links.** `MultiCombobox` (`components/ui/multi-combobox.tsx`): ARIA combobox + listbox, chips with "Remove requirement <title>" buttons, client-side typeahead (the requirements list has no search parameter, so the project's first 100 are fetched). Used by the new Linked Requirements card on case detail (needs `requirement.manage` to edit; read-only chips link to the requirement). One minimal backend addition: `GET /test-cases/{id}/requirements` (id + title), because no case-side listing existed.

**Create and start.** New Test Run shows a primary "Create and start" (users with `executionstep.record`) that opens the Execution Console on the run's first case; "Create Test Run" stays as the secondary and keeps its old behaviour (opens the run page). The last run type is remembered per user in localStorage.

**Skipped.** Requirement-side "Link test cases" mirror (needs its own case picker), combobox inside the create forms (links are made on the detail page), assignee/environment memory (runs have neither field), defect-to-requirement link (no endpoint; a defect already links to its case).

## ADR-107: Page states and headers: one shared pattern

**Context.** After ADR-103 to ADR-106 the main working pages still differed in how they showed "nothing here", "failed to load" and "loading": plain grey text, `FormError`, or nothing at all.

**Decision.** Every main working page follows the same four rules, built from the existing shared pieces (no new components or dependencies).
- **Header.** `PageHeader`: one `<h1>`, an optional one-line description, the single primary create action rightmost (ADR-103), secondary actions to its left, wrapping below the title on phones. Breadcrumbs stay in the AppShell.
- **Empty.** `EmptyState` with a short message saying what the page is for and at most ONE action ("Create your first test case"), rendered only when the user holds the permission. `EmptyState` gained a compatible `variant` prop: `'filtered'` (rows exist but none match) uses a different icon, is marked `data-state="filtered"` and carries a "Clear filters" action. Existing call sites are unchanged.
- **Error.** `ErrorState` with "Try again"; the server's reason is kept in `message`; a 403 renders "No access" instead (existing behaviour).
- **Loading.** Skeleton rows or cards that match the final layout. `ResponsiveTable` now marks its loading table `aria-busy="true"` and its phone card skeleton `role="status"` with a screen-reader "Loading" label.

**Pages changed.** Projects, Test Cases, Test Repository, Test Runs (overview and project list), Defects (filtered variant and Clear filters), Users and Roles, Access Rules. Requirements, Traceability, Reporting, Report Builder, Dashboards, Audit Logs, Roles and Permissions, Settings and the detail pages already followed the pattern.

**Skipped.** Environments, Account and Settings sub-cards (small inline lists inside a card, not page-level states); the Audit Logs "No audit entries found" text (already has skeleton and ErrorState); Requirements empty state (no create action: creation lives in the header and a second action would duplicate it).


## ADR-108: Navigation consolidation: Documentation footer link, Report builder tab

**Decision.** Two sidebar items were removed to shorten the menu. (1) The one-item "Help" group is gone: **Documentation** (`/brd`) is a pinned footer link directly above Profile, styled like Profile, still gated by `canAccessPath`, with the same tooltip in the collapsed rail; the phone More sheet lists it above Profile. (2) The **Report builder** sidebar item is gone: Reports and Report builder are now two tabs (`ReportTabs`, `nav` "Report sections", same pattern as `ProjectTabs`) at the top of `/reporting` and `/reporting/builder`.

**What stays.** The `/reporting/builder` route, breadcrumb and page title are unchanged; the Reports sidebar item (prefix match) is active on both pages; Testers with Reports hidden see neither (routeAccess.ts untouched). The command palette keeps explicit "Report builder" and "Documentation" entries (`PALETTE_EXTRA_ENTRIES`).

**Follow-up (approved).** Roles & Permissions (`/admin/roles`) and Access Rules (`/admin/access-rules`) now share one Administration sidebar item, **Access control** (icon KeyRound). The item shows when the user can reach either route (`canAccessPath`, `routeAccess.ts` untouched), links to the first reachable one, and is active on both; the phone More sheet and collapsed-rail tooltip follow the same sections. A tab strip (`Access control sections`, `routes/admin/AccessControlTabs.tsx`, the ReportTabs pattern) appears on both pages when both are reachable and is omitted when only one is. Both routes, pages, breadcrumbs, titles and permissions are unchanged; the command palette keeps explicit Roles & Permissions and Access Rules entries.

## ADR-110: Overview dashboard redesign and KPI previous-period baseline

The built-in Overview (`/`) now has four equal KPI cards (Pass rate, Active runs, Open defects, Test cases). Each whole card is one link (`qth-card-interactive`, with an `aria-label` of label, value and trend): Pass rate to `/reporting` (`/test-runs` when reports are hidden), Active runs to `/test-runs?status=in_progress`, Open defects to `/defects` (there is no "not closed" filter, so it is unfiltered), Test cases to `/test-repository`. The large gauge is replaced by a compact progress ring inside the Pass rate card. Below sit Execution trend (2/3, 7d/30d/90d) and Test case status (1/3, at most five slices, the rest folded into "Other"), then Needs attention (open Critical/High defects worst-first, then paused runs) and Recent activity (initials, action, entity link, relative time; "View all" to Audit Logs only when `canAccessPath` allows it). With no execution data the trend panel shows a short empty state with a permission-aware "Start a test run" action. The project filter is a Combobox (same `#dashboard-project` id and `?project=` URL); the "Manage dashboards" link became a ghost "Customize" link to the same `/dashboards` page. Custom and project dashboards and the widget catalogue are untouched.

**Trend baseline.** `GET /dashboard/summary` gains an optional `previous` object (`days` = 30, `test_cases_total`, `active_runs`, `open_defects`, `step_pass_percentage`, `step_sample_size`): the state 30 days ago, computed with exactly the same tenant, project-membership, optional `project_id` and tester-visibility scoping as the current figures (cases and runs by `created_at`/`finished_at`, defects by `created_at`/`closed_at`, steps by `closed_at`). The UI shows an arrow, a percentage (percentage points for pass rate) and text, never colour alone; with no baseline (missing `previous`, zero count, no executed steps) it shows "No trend yet" rather than a made-up number. Defect history uses `closed_at`, so a defect reopened after closing counts as open in the baseline only if `closed_at` was cleared.

## ADR-111: List pages redesign: one toolbar, status tabs with counts, row detail drawer

Defects, Test Cases (the repository and each project's list), Test Runs (overview and per project) and Projects now share one layout built from `components/list`: `PageHeader` with one primary action (the secondary ones, such as Import, Export, Manage Modules, live in a "..." menu), status tabs with counts, a `ListToolbar` (search, a Filters popover holding every filter with an active count, removable filter chips with "Clear all", saved views and the column/density menu), the table, and a "Showing 1-25 of 132" footer beside the existing pager.
