On a phone the same filter controls open in the existing Filters bottom sheet. Clicking a table row (not a link, checkbox or button) or pressing Enter on a focused row opens a read-only detail `Drawer` built from the row already loaded, with an "Open full page" link; the title stays a normal link. Priority and severity are a dot plus label (`LevelBadge`); status is the soft `StatusBadge` pill. Low-priority columns start hidden (`defaultHidden` in the column prefs) so tables fit 1280px.

**Backend additions (small, scoped like the existing list endpoints).** `GET /defects` and `GET /defects/status-counts` accept `search` (case-insensitive match on the title, LIKE wildcards escaped), applied inside the shared `_defects_query`, so the page, the tab counts and tester visibility stay consistent. `GET /test-runs` accepts `status` (`in_progress` = not finished and not paused, `paused`, `finished`) and `search` (run name), under the same tenant and project-visibility rules; the tab counts are one-row requests per status. Test case and project status counts reuse the existing list endpoints with `page_size=1` (only `total` is read) under the page's other filters; no new count endpoint was needed. Covered by `tests/test_paged_listings.py`.

## ADR-112: Admin area: one layout with a left sub-nav

The sidebar has a single **Admin** item (ADR-108 follow-up). The pages behind it now share `routes/admin/AdminLayout.tsx`, a React Router layout route wrapped around the existing admin routes, so no URL changed. `components/AdminSubNav.tsx` renders the sections from `lib/adminSections.ts` (Users & Roles, Access Control, Environments, Settings, Audit Logs, grouped as People & access / Workspace / Compliance): a sticky left column from lg up, and a Combobox "Admin section" switcher below lg. The active item has a tint, a left accent bar and `aria-current="page"`. Visibility reuses `canAccessPath`, so permissions are unchanged; a user who can open only one section (a Manager: Environments) gets the page without a lone-item nav. `/settings` stays a top-level route (not moved) and is a section of the area; the sidebar Admin item is active on it as before. Access Control keeps `AccessControlTabs` as its in-page tabs (ADR-108). The temporary `AdminSectionNav` strip is removed. Breadcrumbs read Dashboard > Admin > Section and browser titles read `Admin › Section`.

## ADR-113: Large-tenant performance: indexes, batched serializers, SQL aggregates, report cache, keyset paging

**Context.** A load-test tenant (200k test cases, 50k runs, 500k run cases, 1M execution steps, 100k defects, 500k audit rows) showed list endpoints at 1-7 s (HTTP 500s at 20 concurrent requests), the dashboard at 9-24 s, and some reports and exports timing out. The causes were measured, not guessed: a sequential scan of `test_run_cases` for every test case row ("last execution", 2.5 billion rows read), about ten tiny queries per serialized row, whole `defects` tables loaded into Python to be counted, sorts of the whole tenant for every page, one uvicorn process with a 15-connection pool, and stock Postgres settings. No behaviour, permission, RLS, audit hash-chain or response-shape change was made; everything below is additive or internal.

**Indexes (migration `b4d6f8a0c2e3`, all built `CONCURRENTLY`, reversible; `pg_trgm` is created if missing and left installed on downgrade).**
- `test_run_cases (tenant_id, test_case_id)` and partial `execution_steps (test_run_case_id, closed_at DESC) WHERE closed_at IS NOT NULL` serve the last-execution lookup (83 ms to 0.3 ms per case); `test_run_cases (assigned_to, test_case_id)` and `test_runs (tenant_id, executed_by)` serve the scoped-Tester visibility clause.
- Sort/keyset indexes: `test_cases (tenant_id, created_at, id)` and `(tenant_id, project_id, created_at, id)`; partial (`deleted_at IS NULL`) `defects (tenant_id[, project_id], created_at DESC, id)` and `test_runs (tenant_id, created_at DESC, id)`; `audit_logs (tenant_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)`, which also serves the chain-head lookup made by every audit write and the chain walk (backward scan). Page 1 of the test-case list went from a 197 ms sort of 200k rows to 0.5 ms.
- FK/filter indexes used by joins, filters and cascades: `test_runs (tenant_id, project_id)`, `defects (tenant_id, status | test_case_id | test_run_id | assigned_to | reported_by)`, `test_cases (tenant_id, assignee_id | module_id)`, `requirement_test_case_links (test_case_id)`, `execution_steps (test_case_version_step_id)`. Sparse columns use partial indexes. The other FKs the audit listed are intentionally not indexed (write cost, no read path).
- GIN trigram indexes on `test_cases.title`, `defects.title`, `requirements.title`, `test_runs.name` for `ILIKE '%term%'`. Defect and run search therefore use `ILIKE ... ESCAPE '/'` (same escaping and results as the old `icontains`, which renders `lower(col) LIKE` and cannot use these indexes).
- Dropped the two exact duplicates of a UNIQUE index (`ix_sso_configs_tenant_id`, `ix_subscriptions_tenant_id`); the single-column `tenant_id` indexes that are only partly redundant were kept (RLS-only scans may rely on them). As elsewhere in this repo the indexes live in migrations only, not in the models' `__table_args__`; `alembic check` reports the same pre-existing drift as before.

**Batched serializers.** `test_case_service.to_out_many`, `defect_service.to_out_many` and `requirement_service.to_out_many` (with `to_out` as a one-element call) resolve users, projects, versions, steps, links, status labels, custom fields, execution evidence and last executions with one `WHERE id = ANY(...)` query per table for the whole page (`services/lookups.py`, `custom_field_service.get_values_for_owners`). The project list batches assigned users, run detail batches case titles and verdict counts, and the test-case CSV export and requirement coverage work in chunks or with one GROUP BY. `set_tenant_context` is therefore issued once per page instead of once per row.

**SQL aggregates.** Defect aging, reopen summary, release readiness and defect leakage aggregate in SQL instead of loading every defect or run-case row (100k defects x 507 B per call before). The dashboard summary reads test cases, defects and step verdicts once each and derives the 30-day baseline from the same pass; domain distribution runs one query per measure for all domains; step-verdict queries no longer join `projects`; the aggregate report only joins `test_cases` when a priority/status filter needs it. Numbers are unchanged (same rules, existing tests green). Step verdicts are still counted from `execution_steps`, not from `test_run_cases.status`, because those are different measures.

**Report cache (`core/ttl_cache.py`).** In-process, per worker, `REPORT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (default 20, hard cap 60, 0 = off). Keys start with the tenant id and contain role, user id and every call argument, so tenants, roles and visibility scopes can never share an entry; hits are deep copies; only successes are cached; concurrent identical misses wait for the first computation. A commit that flushed rows of a table the reports read (not sessions, audit rows, notifications, ...) drops that tenant's entries, and an ORM bulk write drops all of them; other workers and the job worker are bounded by the TTL, so a figure can be up to the TTL stale there. Cached: dashboard summary and domain distribution, aggregate, execution and defect trend, defect aging, reopen, release readiness, workload, flaky tests, quality metrics, defect leakage, traceability matrix. The test suite runs with the cache off (`REPORT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS=0`) and covers it in `tests/test_perf_optimisations.py`.

**Pagination (additive).** Page sizes were already capped (test cases, projects, requirements, defects and runs 100; audit 200). New optional `cursor` on `GET /test-cases`, `/projects/{id}/test-cases`, `/defects` (with `page_size`) and `/audit-logs`: an empty value starts keyset paging; the response then carries `next_cursor` (in the body for test cases and defects, in the `X-Next-Cursor` header for the audit list, which stays a bare list); `page`/`offset` are ignored in cursor mode; test cases support `sort=created_at` or `-created_at` only (422 otherwise); a malformed cursor is a 422. `include_total=false` skips the COUNT and returns `total = -1`. Without `cursor` the responses keep their exact old shape. The unpaged `GET /defects` (no `page_size`) is now capped at 5,000 rows (header `X-Result-Truncated: true` when hit); it used to return every defect (100k rows, 50 s).

**Runtime.** `WEB_CONCURRENCY` uvicorn workers (dev 2, prod 4) with per-worker pools (dev 8+8, prod 6+6, kept well under `max_connections`), `DB_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 30 s for the API, 0 for the job worker), `DB_POOL_PRE_PING` (default on), gzip for responses of 1 KB and up, and a 30 s uvicorn keep-alive. Dev compose only: `shared_buffers=256MB`, `work_mem=8MB`, `effective_cache_size=1GB`, `random_page_cost=1.1`, `jit=off`, and `shm_size: 512mb` (Docker's 64 MB `/dev/shm` overflowed under concurrent parallel hash joins and surfaced as HTTP 500 "No space left on device").

**Caveat: rate limits are per worker.** slowapi's storage is in memory, so with N workers the per-IP limits (login, MFA, imports, the 300/min backstop) can be reached N times over in the worst case. The DB-backed lockouts (wrong password, MFA attempts) are unaffected. Moving the limiter store to the database or a shared cache is a follow-up; until then a deployment that needs the exact limits runs `WEB_CONCURRENCY=1`.

**Not done / follow-ups.** `audit_logs` partitioning and retention jobs (sessions, MFA challenges, notifications) are deferred. `verify-chain` still re-hashes every row in the request (about 36 s for 500k rows; `?since_checkpoint=true` is the fast path); a worker job would need a result-polling UI. Non-default test-case sorts (`title`, `priority`, `status`) and `ILIKE` terms that match a large share of rows still cost 150-600 ms at 200k cases. Uncached `execution-trend`, `quality-metrics` and domain distribution scan the 1M-row step table (about 0.5-1.3 s); a per-run verdict rollup table would remove that. `GET /projects/{id}/test-runs` is still an unpaged list.

## ADR-114: Documentation is platform-admin only; tenants get a role-based User Guide

**Context.** The in-app Documentation viewer (`/brd`, ADR-072/089/108) was open to every signed-in tenant user and listed architecture, deployment and engineering references (the sensitive set only to tenant Admins). Those documents describe how the platform is built, secured and operated, which is the platform operator's concern, not a customer's.

**Decision.** (1) The full documentation now lives in the platform console. `GET /api/v1/platform/docs` and `GET /api/v1/platform/docs/{slug}` (platform session, `mfa_verified`, same registry, same slug pattern, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, same rate limits) return every document with no audience filter. The console has a **Documentation** button opening `/platform-admin/docs` and `/platform-admin/docs/:docId`, which reuse `DocsViewer` and `MarkdownView` with injected fetchers and a base path (mermaid, `docs.css` and internal document links work under that route). (2) The tenant app has no Documentation entry any more: the sidebar link, the mobile More item, the palette entry, the `/brd` routes and their breadcrumb/page-title cases are gone; `/brd` and `/brd/*` redirect to `/user-guide`. The tenant router for `GET /docs` and `/docs/{slug}` was removed, so those paths answer 404 for every role and no tenant token can read any document through them. A tenant session cookie does not authenticate `/platform/docs` (401). (3) New tenant page **User Guide** (`/user-guide`, sidebar footer link, More menu, palette; open to every signed-in user, so no `routeAccess.ts` entry). `GET /api/v1/user-guide` returns `{sections: [{id, title, audience, markdown}]}` built from `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (bundled as before by `npm run docs:sync`) split at its level-2 headings by `services/user_guide_service.py`. Sections and what they need: `getting-started` and `tester` (any signed-in user), `manager` (`project.view.all`, the permission `isManagerOrAbove` uses for custom roles), `admin` (`tenant.settings.manage`, the `/settings` gate). A section the caller may not read is not returned or listed, so nothing leaks through the payload. `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. The page shows a left section list from lg up and a Combobox below, opens the section matching the user's role (`#tester`, `#manager`, `#admin`, `#getting-started` deep-link when allowed), with skeleton, empty and error states. The Administrator Guide, deployment, security and architecture content stay platform-only and are not part of the User Guide.

**Consequences.** Supersedes the tenant-facing parts of ADR-072, ADR-089 and ADR-108 (the registry, the no-store header, the path-safe slug and the synced copies are unchanged). Tests: `backend/tests/test_docs_api.py` (tenant `/docs*` serve nothing for Admin, Manager and Tester; `/user-guide` filtered per role; platform admin reads all; no platform session or a tenant session cannot), vitest for the section helpers and page titles, and `e2e/user-guide.spec.ts`.

## ADR-115: 22-field Test Case and Defect standard, step 1: data model, display ids, execution-record rule

**Context.** Test cases and defects are being brought to a fixed 22-field standard (human ids `TC_000001` / `BUG_000001`, test type, severity, automation status, preconditions, postconditions, overall expected result, resolution, labels, build found, fix version and so on) with an eleven-status defect workflow. This step is data model and plumbing only: nothing in the API changes behaviour except that responses gain optional fields and every new test case and defect receives a display id. The earlier gap analysis found that the platform had no human ids, no execution-level actual result, and a defect status CHECK of only five codes.

**Decision.** (1) **Migration `c1a7e3f9b5d2`** (one reversible migration after ADR-113's `b4d6f8a0c2e3`) is purely additive. New table `entity_counters(tenant_id, kind, next_value)` (kind `test_case` or `defect`, UNIQUE per tenant and kind, RLS policy and grants like every tenant table). New nullable columns: `test_cases` (`display_seq`, `display_id`, `test_type`, `severity`, `automation_status` default `Manual`, `environment`, `requirement_ref`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`), `test_case_versions` (`postconditions`, `expected_result`), `test_case_version_steps.test_data`, `test_run_cases` (`actual_result`, `executed_by`, `executed_at`), `execution_steps.actual_result`, `defects` (`display_seq`, `display_id`, `preconditions`, `resolution`, `labels text[]`, `repro_steps jsonb`, `build_found`, `fix_version`, `root_cause_notes`, `test_run_case_id`), `saved_views.columns jsonb`. `ck_defects_status` is recreated with eleven codes (legacy `Open` and `Verified` stay valid; new: Assigned, In Progress, Retest, Reopened, Rejected, Deferred), `ck_test_runs_type` also allows `Retest`, `ck_field_options_field_key` accepts `test_type` and `test_case_severity`, and new CHECKs cover `automation_status` and `defects.resolution`. A trigger makes `display_id` and `display_seq` immutable once set. Backfill is set-based raw SQL (so soft-deleted defects count): `display_seq` is `row_number()` per tenant ordered by `created_at, id`, the counters are set past the maximum, and `defects.test_run_case_id` is derived only where exactly one run case matches the (run, case) pair. **Migration `d2b8f4a0c6e3`** builds the indexes CONCURRENTLY inside `autocommit_block` (UNIQUE `(tenant_id, display_id)` on both tables as partial indexes, test type and automation status filters, labels GIN, `test_run_cases(executed_by)`, `defects(test_run_case_id)`); `defects(tenant_id, status)` already existed from ADR-113. The downgrade of the first migration maps the new defect statuses back (Assigned, In Progress, Reopened to Open; Retest to Fixed; Rejected, Deferred to Closed) and `Retest` runs to `Regression` before restoring the old CHECKs, then drops the columns, trigger and table. (2) **Display ids**: `services/display_id_service.py` reserves a range with one atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... RETURNING` on `entity_counters`; concurrent creators block on the counter row until commit and so always receive disjoint ranges, a rolled-back create returns its numbers, and bulk inserts (test case and defect CSV import) reserve `n` numbers in one statement. Ids are assigned only on create, never accepted by any update schema, and never reused (soft-deleted rows keep theirs). (3) **Execution-record rule**: execution-specific data (actual result, executed by, execution date, execution status) lives on the execution records (`test_run_cases`, `execution_steps`), never on the test case definition, which stays an authoring document with immutable versions (ADR-003). The test case list keeps deriving its "last execution" read-only. Versioned content (`postconditions`, `expected_result`, step `test_data`) lives on the version tables, metadata (type, severity, automation status, environment) on `test_cases`. (4) **Value sets**: Test Type is a tenant-configurable `field_options` key (`test_type`, twelve defaults); test case severity is the new key `test_case_severity` (Critical, Major, Minor); `defect_severity` gains Major and Minor additively; legacy codes stay valid and no row is rewritten (the High, Medium, Low priority sets already exist). Automation Status, defect status and resolution are fixed code-validated enums in `core/field_standards.py`, together with `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` and the status-to-resolution table as pure data (not enforced yet). Existing tenants are seeded by the migration, new tenants by `field_option_service.seed_defaults_for_tenant`. (5) **Permissions**: new codes `defect.edit`, `defect.assign`, `defect.change_status`, `defect.import`, `defect.export`, `testcase.execute`, `testcase.export`, granted to the holders of the nearest existing equivalent (`defect.create`, `defect.close`, `executionstep.record`, `testcase.bulk_import_export`, including custom roles) plus Admin and Manager, so no role gains access it did not have; none is enforced yet. (6) **Reserved keys**: custom field definitions may not use the key of a fixed standard field (refused on create; existing definitions are untouched and listed by `scripts/custom_field_clash_scan.py`). (7) Soft delete: `test_cases.deleted_at` is covered by the existing ORM hook; the delete path itself is unchanged in this step.

**Consequences.** Later steps (validation of the mandatory fields on create and import only, the defect transition table and resolution rules, retest runs, execution actual result, soft delete of test cases, preview-then-commit import and the UI) build on these columns. Rows created by raw SQL outside the services (for example `scripts/seed_loadtest.py`) have no display id until backfilled. Tests: `backend/tests/test_display_ids.py` (sequential ids, immutability, per-tenant isolation and RLS, bulk ranges, concurrent allocation, status CHECK with new and legacy codes, reserved keys, and a migration up/down/up on a scratch database with backfill assertions).

## ADR-116: 22-field Test Case standard, step 2: Test Case backend

**Context.** ADR-115 added the columns, display ids and permission codes. This step makes the Test Case API use them: mandatory fields on create and import, the new fields on every read, derived execution status, the execution record (actual result, executor, date), soft delete, duplicate, richer list filters / search / sorts, the column catalogue, export of all fields and before/after audit. Backend only; the UI follows in later steps.

**Decision.**

1. **Fields.** Create / update / out carry the 22-field structure. Content (description, preconditions, test data, steps, expected result, postconditions) lives on `test_case_versions` and follows ADR-003 / ADR-020 unchanged (a content edit to an approved case forks a new version and reverts to draft; older versions and their steps are never touched; a draft is edited in place). Metadata (module, priority, assignee, test type, severity, automation status, environment, requirement reference) lives on `test_cases` and never forks a version. `preconditions` is the new name of `prerequisite`; both are accepted on input (a different value in each is a 422) and both are returned. Steps: 1 to 200; `step_no` sent by a client is ignored and steps are renumbered 1..n from list order (a reorder is the same list in a new order); a step may carry `test_data`; `expected_result` is accepted as an alias of a step's `expected`. Step `expected` stays required (the database CHECK `ck_tcv_steps_expected_not_empty` is untouched), so the case-level expected result is mandatory and a step's own expected result is required too.
2. **Mandatory on create, in the API only.** `module_id`, `title`, `priority`, `preconditions`, `test_data`, at least one step and the case-level `expected_result`. Title, priority and steps are checked by the request schema; module, preconditions, test data and expected result by `test_case_service.enforce_create_requirements` called from the route, so internal creators (AI generation, templates, sample data) are unaffected. Every 422 carries `error.fields` (`{field: message}`); the generic validation handler now lists every offending field this way. Reads never validate. A PATCH of a legacy row is not refused because the row has empty mandatory fields: only an edit that clears a value the row has (module, preconditions, test data, expected result, priority) is a 422. Limits: title 5-255, description 20,000, preconditions / postconditions / expected result 10,000, test data 20,000, step text 2,000, step data 5,000, 200 steps.
3. **Text hygiene.** Markdown fields are stored as Markdown text: `<script>` / `<style>` blocks, tags and comments are stripped on the way in (`core/text_sanitize.py`), control characters removed. Test data is free text; `test_data_format` is a request hint (`auto`, `text`, `json`): only `json` rejects malformed JSON. Responses return `test_data_format` (`json` for an object or array, else `text`).
4. **Value sets.** Test Type comes from the tenant's `field_options` key `test_type`; the default list is exactly Functional, UI, Smoke, Regression, Integration, System, End-to-End, Security, Performance, Compatibility, Usability, API (new tenants are seeded with it). **Migration `e4c8a1d7f3b9`** (data only, reversible) adds UI and End-to-End for existing tenants and sets the order; Sanity and UAT seeded by step 1 are kept (not deleted: `field_options` has no active flag) and sort after the twelve. Severity uses `test_case_severity` (Critical, Major, Minor); Automation Status is the fixed enum Manual / Automated / Candidate for Automation (default Manual). A value a case already has may be re-sent unchanged even if its option was later removed.
5. **Status names.** The lifecycle `status` (draft, ready_for_review, approved, deprecated) keeps its database and API name; its product label is "Review Status" and `review_status` / `review_status_label` repeat it. `execution_status` is a different, derived field.
6. **Derived execution status.** From the latest *executed* run case (status passed, failed, blocked or ignored, in a live run; legacy rows fall back to `completed_at` / `started_at` and the last step executor): passed = Pass, failed = Fail, blocked = Blocked, ignored = Skipped, none = Not Run. A newer run case that is still pending or in progress does not hide the last real result (a deviation from reading "pending = Not Run" literally for the latest run case; Not Run means never executed). With `last_executed_by`, `last_executed_at` and `last_actual_result`; nothing is stored on the test case (ADR-115 execution-record rule).
7. **Execution record.** `VerdictRequest.actual_result` and `BlockRunCaseRequest.actual_result` are optional text (10,000). When a run case reaches passed or failed it is stamped `executed_by` / `executed_at`; blocking stamps them too (unblocking clears them); CI results stamp the time only. The failed step's actual result is copied to the run case. Rule for the actual result: **required for `fail` only when the setting `REQUIRE_ACTUAL_RESULT_ON_FAIL` is on (default off)**, optional for pass and ignore; blocking already requires a reason. It is off by default because the existing Execution Console does not send the field yet; the UI step turns it on. Re-recording a verdict while a run is open still replaces the step's current verdict, but the audit event (`executionstep.verdict_recorded`) now records `changes: {verdict, actual_result, run_case_status}` with from / to, so no earlier result is lost; block, unblock and CI events record status from / to too. Executing never writes to the test case definition. `GET /test-cases/{id}/executions` lists one row per run case for the history tab (run, date, executor, status, actual result, defects, version number), paged. Recording needs `executionstep.record` OR `testcase.execute`; access rules are evaluated against the canonical action `executionstep.record`.
8. **Soft delete.** `DELETE /test-cases/{id}` sets `deleted_at` / `deleted_by`; the existing ORM hook (`models/soft_delete.py`) removes the row from every ORM query, so lists, search, counts, module counts, reports, the CSV / XLSX export, import duplicate-title checks and requirement coverage / traceability exclude it, while raw `text()` SQL and `include_deleted` reads (tenant data export, project deletion guard) still see it. The guard that refused deletion once runs or defects referenced the case is removed: versions, run cases, defects, comments and attachments are kept, so history stays readable by id (the history and executions endpoints work for Admin and Manager; the case itself, detail and edits are 404; a Tester gets 404 for everything). Run listings, run case context and defect views name the deleted case from an `include_deleted` read; re-running failed cases skips it; a project that only holds deleted cases still cannot be deleted. The display id is never reused. Permission: `testcase.delete` (project-role overrides apply); a Tester-tier caller without it may still delete their own draft, as before; built-in Admin and Manager hold the code, so their behaviour is unchanged, while a custom role mapped to the manager tier now needs the code instead of passing an inline role check.
9. **Duplicate.** `POST /test-cases/{id}/duplicate` (`testcase.create`) copies the definition into a new draft, version 1, with a new display id and the title suffixed ` (Copy)`; no executions, defects, comments, attachments, requirement links, custom field values, assignee or reviewer.
10. **Listing.** New filters `test_type`, `severity`, `automation_status`, `environment`, `execution_status`, `executed_by`; `search` matches display id, title, requirement reference or module name with ILIKE (LIKE wildcards in the term are literal) served by trigram indexes; sorts by `display_id` (numeric), `test_type`, `priority`, `severity`, `automation_status`, `last_executed_at`, `execution_status`. Execution filters and sorts join one DISTINCT ON subquery over the executed run cases (partial index `ix_test_run_cases_executed_case`). Keyset cursors still work for `created_at` sorts only. **Migration `f7a3d9c1e5b2`** builds the indexes CONCURRENTLY (display_seq order, trigram on display id, requirement reference and module name, severity, executed run cases). `GET /test-cases/columns` lists the 22 fields with labels, types and flags and the nine default columns (Test Case ID, Title, Module, Test Type, Priority, Automation Status, Execution Status, Executed By, Last Execution Date) for the column picker; module paths use the existing `test_modules` nesting, which has no depth limit (the display walks it with a cycle guard).
11. **Export and import.** The CSV / XLSX export carries all 22 fields (display id, requirement, module, type, severity, automation, environment, preconditions, test data, steps with optional per-step data, expected result, postconditions, execution status, actual result, executed by, execution date, defect ids, created by, attachment and comment counts) plus the legacy columns; `columns=` selects and orders them (unknown or repeated key = 422); `testcase.export` or `testcase.bulk_import_export` is accepted. "Actual Result" is the latest recorded text, falling back to the last verdict for executions that predate actual results. Import accepts the 13 legacy headers optionally followed by Requirement ID, Test Type, Severity, Automation Status, Environment, Expected Result, Postconditions; a file with any of the new columns must fill Prerequisite, Test Data and Expected Result on every row (mandatory fields apply on import), the legacy 13-column file keeps its old rules, and steps may be `Action|Expected|Test data`. The template, xlsx reading and preview stay for step 6.
12. **Audit.** `testcase.created` and `testcase.updated` carry `changes: {field: {from, to}}` (text truncated to 300 characters; steps summarised; the hash chain covers the metadata as before). New events: `testcase.duplicated`, `testcase.deleted` (soft, with display id), `testcase.requirement_linked` / `testcase.requirement_unlinked` (on the case's history, next to the existing requirement events), execution changes as above.
13. **RBAC.** Create `testcase.create`, edit `testcase.edit`, delete `testcase.delete` (own draft for a Tester), duplicate `testcase.create`, execute `executionstep.record` OR `testcase.execute`, export `testcase.bulk_import_export` OR `testcase.export`. ADR-115 granted the new codes to the holders of the old ones, so built-in Admin / Manager / Tester behaviour is unchanged; only a custom role that relied on the manager tier for deletion is affected.

**Consequences.** Clients that create test cases through the API must send the new mandatory fields (the shipped create form does not yet; it is updated in the UI step). The Execution Console works unchanged because the actual-result requirement is off by default. Tests: `backend/tests/test_test_case_standard.py` (mandatory fields, limits, versioning, immutability, legacy rows, audit, soft delete visibility across list, search, coverage, export and project deletion, execution status mapping, executions history, actual result and re-verdict history, duplicate, filters / search / sorts / cursor, column catalogue, export columns, import, RBAC matrices for built-in and custom roles, RLS isolation, tester visibility, the test-type migration up / down / up); `tests/conftest.py` gains a shared builder that fills the new mandatory fields for older tests (send `X-Test-Strict-Create: 1` to talk to the API unmodified).

## ADR-117: 22-field Test Case standard, step 3: Test Case UI, saved-view columns, Actual Result on fail

**Context.** ADR-116 made the API require the mandatory fields on create (the shipped create form was getting 422s), added the derived execution status, the executions history, duplicate and soft delete. This step is the UI for it, plus the two small backend changes it needs.

**Decision.**

1. **Create / edit form.** A full page (`/projects/:id/test-cases/new`, `/test-cases/:id/edit`) replaces the drawer form: six collapsible sections (Basic Information, Classification, Test Preparation, Test Execution Instructions, Execution Details, Additional Information), required fields marked and validated on blur, the API's `error.fields` mapped back onto the fields (the section that holds a failing field opens and shows a count), entries kept on failure, and a dirty guard (Cancel, in-app links and tab close). Module / Sub-module / Feature are cascading selects over the existing module tree (the deepest choice is `module_id`; a project with no module can create one inline). Description, preconditions, expected result and postconditions are Markdown with a Preview tab (react-markdown, no raw HTML, the API strips HTML). Test Data accepts text or JSON with a validity hint, pretty-print and an optional "must be valid JSON" switch (`test_data_format=json`). Execution Details is read-only (latest execution from the derived fields, link to the history) with a note that executing happens in a run. **Save as Draft** creates the case (new cases are always Draft); **Save & Submit for Review** also calls submit-review; editing sends only the changed fields so a metadata-only edit stays metadata-only. The scenario fields of the old form are kept under Additional Information. Quick add now asks for every field the API requires (title, module, priority, preconditions, test data, expected result, steps) and hands the entries to the full form via "More options".
2. **Dynamic Step Builder** (`components/StepBuilder.tsx`, logic in `lib/stepBuilder.ts`): a field-set prop (`testcase`, `defect`) so defect reproduction steps can reuse it; automatic numbering by position, Action, optional Test data, required Expected result, add, inline edit, remove with an Undo toast, drag reorder (native HTML5 drag from a handle) plus Move up / Move down buttons and Arrow keys on the handle as the WCAG 2.5.7 alternative, pasting several lines creates a step per line, at most 200 steps. No new dependency.
3. **List.** Default columns are the nine of `GET /test-cases/columns`; the picker has three modes (Default, All fields = the 22, Custom) driven by that catalogue, with Review Status and Assignee as extra optional columns. The old lifecycle "Status" label is "Review Status" in the UI everywhere (tabs, filter, column, detail); Execution Status is a separate pill with an icon and a label. Filters cover test type, priority, severity, automation status, environment, execution status, executed by, module, review status, assignee and quarantine; the tabs stay Review Status tabs (the dashboard deep link `?status=` keeps working). Page size 25, "Showing a-b of n". Row menu: Open, Edit, Duplicate (opens the copy), Delete (confirmation; soft). Buttons follow `testcase.create` / `edit` / `delete` / `export`; the backend remains the authority. The cross-project Test Repository gets the Test Case ID, Execution Status and Review Status columns and sends New Test Case to the same form; the full column modes and saved views live on the per-project list.
4. **Saved views carry columns.** `saved_views.columns` (added in ADR-115) is now writable: `SavedViewCreateRequest` / `SavedViewUpdateRequest` take `columns`, an ordered list of at most 40 keys of shape `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*` (shape only: which keys exist is the list's own catalogue and an unknown key is ignored when a view is applied), de-duplicated; PATCH with `columns: null` clears it. A saved view therefore stores filters, sort and columns; the browser's own column choice (localStorage, per user) stays as the fallback when a view has none. The Test Case filter allowlist already held the ADR-116 keys.
5. **Export of the filtered set.** `GET /projects/{id}/test-cases/export.csv|xlsx` accept the list's filters and `sort`, applied through the same `_filter_sort_page` the list uses (paged internally), so "export the current view" can never disagree with the list. With no filters the previous query is used unchanged. The export menu offers Excel and CSV with all fields or with `columns=` set to the shown columns.
6. **Actual Result is now required to fail.** The Execution Console has an inline Actual Result box (required to fail a step, optional for Pass, none for Ignore; Blocked keeps its reason dialog); the re-run control has the same box. With that in place `REQUIRE_ACTUAL_RESULT_ON_FAIL` defaults to **true** (config, `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.prod.yml`, `.env.example`); API clients that cannot send `actual_result` can set it to false. Existing tests and e2e helpers that fail a step now send an actual result; a test keeps proving that the setting off still allows it.
7. **Detail page.** Tabs: Details, Steps, Executions (run, date, executor, status pill, actual result, version, defects, from `GET /test-cases/{id}/executions`), Versions, Comments & Attachments, History (the audit events with the old and new value of every changed field).

**Consequences.** The web create path works against ADR-116 again. API clients keep the mandatory-field rule and now also need `actual_result` on a failing verdict unless the setting is turned off. A saved view without columns behaves as before. Test Case export now needs the list's own filter parameter names (unchanged for callers that send none).

## ADR-118: 22-field Test Case and Defect standard, step 4: Defect backend

**Context.** ADR-115 added the defect columns, display ids and permission codes; ADR-116 brought the Test Case API to the standard. This step makes the Defect API use them: mandatory fields, the eleven-status workflow with resolution rules, one link model between defects and test cases, defects raised from failed executions, a retest workflow, enforced permissions, richer list / search / export and full before / after audit. Backend only; the UI follows in a later step.

**Decision.**

1. **Fields and validation.** Create, update and read carry the 22 fields. Mandatory on **create, in the API only** (422 with `error.fields`, one message per field, every offending field at once): title (5-255), description, at least one step to reproduce, expected result, actual result, environment (text or `environment_id`), severity, priority. Status defaults to New (New or Assigned-with-an-assignee only); the reporter is the caller and is never read from the request; the date is `created_at`. The check runs after whatever an execution supplies is filled in, and internal creators are unaffected. Limits: description 20,000, preconditions / expected / actual / root cause notes 10,000, step action 2,000, step data 5,000, 200 steps, environment / build / fix version 200, labels at most 20 of 40 characters (trimmed, lower-cased, case-insensitively de-duplicated). Markdown fields are stored as Markdown with HTML stripped on the way in. The assignee must be an **active** user of the tenant and a member of the defect's project. **PATCH** follows the Test Case rules: a legacy row is never refused for a mandatory value it already lacks, only a request that blanks a value the row has is a 422. Expected and actual result are editable after creation. `display_id` and `reported_by` are not in any request schema; the database trigger from ADR-115 keeps the id immutable. Steps are stored in `defects.repro_steps` (`step_no`, `action`, `test_data`, `expected_result`, optional `failed`), renumbered 1..n from list order, and the legacy `steps_to_reproduce` text is re-rendered as numbered lines on every write; the legacy text sent alone is converted to steps, and a legacy row reads back with steps derived from its text.
2. **Status workflow.** `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` in `core/field_standards.py` is now enforced by `defect_service.apply_transition`, the single place a status changes (status endpoint, reopen, retest result). Adjusted from the ADR-115 data: In Progress may also go to Rejected, Retest may go to Verified, Deferred may go back to New. The lifecycle is New -> Assigned -> In Progress -> Fixed -> Retest -> Closed with Retest / Closed / Rejected -> Reopened, New / Assigned / In Progress -> Rejected / Deferred, Deferred -> Assigned / New, Reopened -> Assigned / In Progress; the legacy sequence New -> Open -> Fixed -> Verified -> Closed is a path through the same table (Open behaves like Assigned / In Progress, Verified is "retest passed, awaiting close"). An invalid move is a **409** naming the allowed next statuses; an unknown code is a 422. Closing keeps the explicit `verification_confirmed` gesture. `closed_at` is stamped on Closed and Rejected and cleared on Reopened; `reopened_count` / `last_reopened_at` count every move into Reopened; the dedicated reopen endpoint maps to Reopened (it used to set Open) and writes `defect.reopened` as before (the status endpoint writes the same event for Reopened).
3. **Resolution rules.** New, Open, Assigned, In Progress, Retest and Reopened must not carry a resolution (a move into them clears it, so Fixed -> Retest and any reopen drop it; sending one is a 422). Fixed defaults to Fixed; Verified may carry Fixed; Rejected needs Duplicate, Won't Fix, Cannot Reproduce, By Design or Not Applicable; Deferred may carry Won't Fix, By Design or Not Applicable; Closed needs one of the six, keeps a valid existing one, and defaults to Fixed when it comes from Fixed, Retest or Verified, so the legacy flow without resolutions still works. `PATCH` may set the resolution, validated against the current status.
4. **Status groups.** `CLOSED_DEFECT_STATUSES` = Closed, Rejected. `OPEN_DEFECT_STATUSES` = New, Open, Assigned, In Progress, Reopened, Retest (used where "open" gates something: release readiness, leakage, test plan roll-up, workload). Fixed, Retest and Verified are "awaiting verification"; Deferred is open but parked. Every count that used `status != 'Closed'` (dashboard, project summaries, aging, compliance report, duplicate detection) now means "not Closed or Rejected"; the closed-per-period count includes Rejected. SLA: severities Major (5 days) and Minor (15) join the legacy four; a Deferred defect ages but never counts as breached and the SLA sweep skips Deferred, Closed and Rejected. Status labels and the report builder list all eleven codes.
5. **Test case links, one source.** The primary `defects.test_case_id` and the `defect_links` rows of type `test_case` are one set: `POST` / `DELETE /defects/{id}/test-cases/{test_case_id}` link and unlink (idempotent, audited with the old and new list), the target must exist in the tenant, not be soft-deleted and belong to the defect's project (404 / 422). Unlinking the primary case clears the FK. `GET /defects/{id}/test-cases` lists display id, title, module path, latest execution status, date and tester, the run and the run case the defect was raised from (a scoped Tester only sees cases assigned to them); `GET /test-cases/{id}/defects` and the existing `TestCaseOut.linked_defects` read the same union, and the list filter `test_case_id` uses it. `defect_links.linkable_type` gains `test_run_case`; the generic add-link endpoint now validates its target, is idempotent and has a remove counterpart. Many defects per case and many cases per defect work.
6. **From a failed execution.** `GET /test-run-cases/{id}/defect-draft` returns a prefilled draft without storing anything (title `TC_000012 – <title> failed`; module; preconditions; the steps up to and including the failing one, which is flagged; expected = the failing step's, else the case's; actual = the execution's actual result; environment = the case's; tester and date; the evidence attachments; the defects already linked). `POST /defects` with `test_run_case_id` (or the existing `execution_step_id`, which keeps its `Step N Failed: <action>` title) fills whatever the request omits from the same derivation, sets `test_run_case_id`, links the case and the run case, and copies the execution's attachments by reference (new rows over the same stored file; deleting one row only removes the file with its last row). `POST /test-run-cases/{id}/defects/{defect_id}` links an existing defect of the same project. **Deviation:** a run has no environment or build of its own, so the draft takes the environment from the test case and leaves the build empty.
7. **Retest.** `POST /defects/{id}/retest` (a Fixed or Retest defect) creates a test run of type `Retest` (now also selectable when creating a run by hand) with the defect's linked cases at their current versions, the run cases assigned to the reporter or `tester_id`, the defect linked to each run case; a Fixed defect moves to Retest and the reporter, assignee and tester are notified. `POST /defects/{id}/retest-result` `{result, run_case_id, comment}` needs the run case to belong to a Retest run of the defect and to show the matching status (passed / failed): pass closes the defect with resolution Fixed when the caller may close (`defect.close` or Admin / Manager), otherwise it moves to Verified (a Tester cannot close, as before); fail moves it to Reopened, increments `reopened_count`, notifies assignee and reporter and appends the failure text to the actual result. Run cases are never modified: every retest is its own run case, `GET /defects/{id}/retests` lists them with their results, and the history carries `defect.retest_started` / `defect.retest_result`.
8. **Permissions** (backend enforced; the new code OR its legacy equivalent, with the project-role override as for other routes): create `defect.create`; edit, labels, links `defect.edit` or `defect.create`; assignee change `defect.assign` or `defect.close`; status change and retest `defect.change_status`, `defect.create` or `defect.close`; close and reopen `defect.close` or the Admin / Manager tier (finally enforced; a retest result uses `defect.change_status` alone); delete `defect.delete`; import `defect.import` or `defect.create`; export `defect.export`. ADR-115 granted the new codes to the holders of the parent codes, so Admin, Manager and Tester keep what they had, except that a Tester can no longer assign (the brief) or export.
9. **Listing, search, export.** Filters: project, status, severity, priority (each multi), assignee, reporter, module, labels (all of), linked test case, date range, fix version, environment, resolution; search over Bug ID, title, description snippet and exact label (trigram indexes); sorts by Bug ID, title, severity, priority, status (rank), assignee, date reported, fix version; keyset cursors keep working for the default order. **Migrations `a9d3f5b7c1e4`** (the `test_run_case` link type; the notification types `defect_assigned`, `defect_reopened`, `defect_retest`) and **`b6e0c2a8d4f7`** (CONCURRENTLY: Bug ID order, trigram on Bug ID and description, severity, priority, fix version, link target lookup). `GET /defects/columns` lists the 22 fields, the ten default columns, sorts, filters, statuses with transitions and export columns. `GET /defects/export.csv|xlsx` (permission `defect.export`) writes all 22 fields plus Root Cause Notes, with `columns=`, `ids=` and the list filters, CSV cells guarded against formula injection, at most 50,000 rows. Saved views accept the new defect filter keys and sorts.
10. **Audit.** Create and update events carry `changes: {field: {from, to}}` (text truncated at 300 characters); assignment, status, reopen, link / unlink, attachment add / remove (now audited for every owner type), retest start and result all have from / to values; the history endpoint lifts them into a `changes` field. Comments were already audited. The hash chain covers the metadata as before. CSV import accepts the new columns optionally (see the API specification); a file using any of them must satisfy the new mandatory rules, the legacy eleven-column file keeps its own.

**Consequences.** Clients that create defects through the API must send the new mandatory fields (the shipped create dialog is updated in the UI step; the test client's shared builder fills them for older tests, `X-Test-Strict-Create: 1` bypasses it). The reopen endpoint now yields status Reopened instead of Open. The legacy status sequence still works unchanged. Tests: `backend/tests/test_defect_standard.py` (mandatory fields, boundaries, assignee validity, immutable ids, editing expected / actual, legacy rows, every one of the 110 status pairs, the legacy flow, resolution rules, reopen, permission matrices for Tester / Manager / Admin and custom roles, link / unlink in both directions, the failed-execution draft and automatic link, linking from a run case, retest pass / fail with history preserved, filters / sorts / search / cursor, the column catalogue, export with `columns=` and `ids=`, saved views, audit before / after, attachments, soft delete, RLS and tester visibility, reports / dashboard / SLA with the new statuses, CSV import); existing tests that asserted the old reopen status, the single run-case link or the run-type restriction were updated.

## ADR-119: 22-field Test Case and Defect standard, step 5: Defect UI

**Context.** ADR-118 made the Defect API require the mandatory fields, enforce an eleven-status workflow with resolution rules, link defects and test cases in one model, raise defects from failed executions and run a retest workflow. The shipped create form and the Execution Console's report dialog were getting 422s. This step is the UI for all of it, built on the patterns of ADR-117.

**Decision.**

1. **Create / edit form** (`/defects/new`, `/defects/:id/edit`; `DefectFormPage`): a full page in six collapsible sections (Bug Overview, Reproduction Details, Environment & Evidence, Classification, Ownership & Tracking, Traceability) built from the shared `FormSection` (extracted from the test case form). Required fields are marked and validated on blur, the API's `error.fields` are mapped onto the fields (the section holding one opens and shows a count), entries survive a failure, and `useUnsavedGuard` guards Cancel, links and tab close. Steps to Reproduce reuse the Step Builder's `defect` field set (a step and an optional expected result; the step that failed in an execution is flagged "Failed here"). Environment, Build and Fix Version use `PickOrType` (pick an environment, build or release, or type one). New shared pieces: `TagInput` (labels, 20 of 40 characters, trimmed and lower-cased as the API stores them), `SearchPicker` (server-side search; `TestCasePicker` and `DefectPicker` are thin wrappers), `PendingAttachments` (files checked against the upload rules, uploaded after the defect is created; a saved defect uses the existing `AttachmentsPanel`), `MultiCheckFilter`, `CommitTextFilter`, `HistoryTimeline` (now also used by the test case history). Create sends everything in one `POST /defects`. Edit sends only changed fields in one `PATCH`; link changes use the link endpoints and a status move uses `PATCH /status` afterwards, in that order, and a failure part-way reloads the saved copy as the new baseline, so nothing is applied twice and what failed stays highlighted. Status on edit lists the current status and the allowed next statuses only (from `GET /defects/columns`, with a built-in copy of the table as fallback); Closed and Reopened are shown disabled with the reason for a user without the close permission; Resolution is shown only where the status allows one, required only where the rules need one and no default exists, with the rule explained beside it; Closed needs the verification tick. The assignee is hidden (read-only with the reason) without `defect.assign`.
2. **Defect from a failed step** (`DefectQuickCreateDialog`): opens on Fail, loads `GET /test-run-cases/{id}/defect-draft` and prefills title, description, module, steps (failing step flagged), expected, actual, environment and tester; severity has no default (a conscious choice) and priority defaults to Medium. The mandatory fields are editable in the dialog, the defect is created with `test_run_case_id` (the server links it to the case and run case and copies the evidence), and **Link Existing Defect** uses a searchable picker and `POST /test-run-cases/{id}/defects/{defect_id}`. The console's Defects panel lists the defects of the run case, raised or linked, as Bug ID chips (from the draft's `existing_defects`, because `GET /defects?test_run_id=` does not match a defect linked only to a run case).
3. **List** (`DefectsList`, one component for `/defects` and a project's tab `/defects?projectId=`): default columns are the ten of the catalogue; the shared column-mode logic (`lib/columnModes.ts`, now behind both the test case and defect lists) offers Default, All fields (22) and Custom, plus the UI-only extras Project and SLA / Age (never exported); saved views carry filters, sort and columns (`list_key=defects`). Filters: status, severity and priority (several at once), assignee, reporter, module, labels, linked test case, date range, fix version, environment, resolution; the search box covers Bug ID, title, description and label. Sorting uses the API's sort fields. Pages hold 50 rows (as in ADR-078; test cases use 25), "Showing a-b of n". **Status tabs** are fixed groups: Open (every status except Closed and Rejected, the backend definition behind the nav badge and the dashboard KPI, whose link now carries those statuses), Fixed (Fixed + Verified), Retest, Reopened, Deferred (parked, still open) and Closed (Closed + Rejected); tabs may overlap. The Export menu offers Excel and CSV with all fields or the shown columns for the filtered set, and for ticked rows (`ids=`, at most 100 ticks). Row menu: Open, Edit, Assign, Change status, Delete (confirmation). A row opens the quick-view drawer. The old "New Defect" dialog and its code are removed.
4. **Status display**: all eleven statuses are an icon plus the tenant's label (`DefectStatusBadge`, `routes/defects/statusDisplay.ts`), never colour alone; the same pill is used on the list, detail, drawer, console, test run, test case and project screens.
5. **Detail page**: header with Bug ID, status pill, severity and priority, and the actions Edit, Assign, Change status (a menu of the allowed next statuses; a dialog asks for the resolution, a comment and the verification tick), Start retest (Fixed or Retest), Reopen, Close (as permissions allow) and Delete. Tabs: Details, Steps, Linked Test Cases (the table of ADR-118 with link another and unlink), Retests (timeline from `GET /defects/{id}/retests`), Comments & Attachments, History (before / after of every change). When a retest run case has been executed and its result is not yet recorded, a banner offers **Retest passed: Close** (Verify when the user may not close) and **Retest failed: Reopen** (`POST /defects/{id}/retest-result`); run cases are never modified and the Retests tab keeps every one. The test case page gets a **Defects** tab (`GET /test-cases/{id}/defects`) with link, unlink and Report defect (the form opens with the case linked).
6. **Permissions** mirror the backend codes through `useDefectPermissions` (create, edit, assign, change status, close, delete, export, each also accepting the legacy equivalent as ADR-118 does); the backend stays the authority and a refusal is shown on the form or in the dialog.
7. **Tests.** Vitest for the form rules and error mapping, the transition menu and resolution rules, the quick actions, the status groups and tabs, the column modes, the tag rules, the status tones and pills, and the Step Builder's defect field set; `e2e/defect-standard.spec.ts` covers create with inline validation, edit after creation, assignment, status transitions with resolutions, link / unlink in both directions, a defect from a failed step and linking an existing one, the retest pass and fail paths, All fields columns and a saved view, export, filters, the Tester's limits, the dirty guard and 1440 / 1280 / 768 / 375 in light and dark. Existing specs that created defects through the API now send the mandatory fields (`seedDefectBody` in `e2e/fixtures.ts`), and the ones that walked the old "Advance to" buttons use the Change status dialog (`changeDefectStatus`).

**Deviations.** The saved-view filter check accepts only letters, digits, spaces, an apostrophe and `_ . -` in a token, so the resolution Won't Fix can be stored in a view; a label with any other character (such as `/` or `:`) still cannot, and the view is refused with a message (nothing else is affected). The Open tab overlaps the Fixed, Retest, Reopened and Deferred tabs on purpose (it is the single "not finished" number used everywhere). Attachments added while creating are uploaded after the defect exists, so a failed upload leaves the defect created with a warning.

**Consequences.** The web create path, the report dialog and every status move work against ADR-118 again. The `Advance to <status>` buttons are gone: moving a defect on is Change status, and the legacy chain New, Open, Fixed, Verified, Closed remains available through it. The old `DefectsList` dialog, inline selects on the detail page and the per-column local filters were removed with their code.


## ADR-120: 22-field Test Case and Defect standard, step 6: import and export

**Context.** Steps 2 to 5 brought both modules to the 22-field standard but import was still the legacy fixed-column CSV (valid rows inserted, bad rows reported, no preview, no Excel) and the exports used the old column labels. This step adds a standard import for both modules and aligns the exports with it.

**Decision.**

1. **Templates.** `GET /test-cases/import-template.xlsx|.csv` and `GET /defects/import-template.xlsx|.csv` (`testcase.bulk_import_export` or `testcase.create`; `defect.import` or `defect.create`). The headers are the labels of `GET /test-cases/columns` / `GET /defects/columns` (the catalogue is the single source: `core/import_columns.py`), followed by the few extra columns of the legacy layout (test cases: Assignee Email, Review Status, Scenario Id, Scenario Description, Project Name; defects: Root Cause Notes). A header row, one example row (skipped on import) and, in the workbook, an `Instructions` sheet (steps format, rules, a per-column table with Required / Optional / Ignored), an `Allowed values` sheet (the tenant's own test type, priority, severity options; automation status; review status; defect statuses with their resolution rules, resolutions and root causes) and dropdown data validation on the enum columns over 5,000 rows. Mandatory columns have an amber header and a comment, derived columns are grey. The CSV template is header plus example.
2. **Steps in one cell.** One step per line, `N. action | test data | expected result`; empty parts keep their bars; a literal bar is `\|`; a line without a number continues the previous step. Chosen over a multi-row-per-case layout because a case stays one row (sortable, filterable, diffable) and the format survives Excel and CSV. The reader also accepts labelled parts (`data:` / `expected:`, what the earlier defect export wrote), an unnumbered one-step-per-line cell and the legacy `Action|Expected[|Data]` cells separated by `;`. A test case step needs an action and an expected result; a defect step only an action.
3. **Reading files.** `services/import_file_service.py`. `.xlsx` through openpyxl 3.1.5 (new pinned dependency; the api, worker images are rebuilt) in read-only mode, values only: formulas (any cell of type formula), macros (`.xlsm`/`.xlsb`/`.xltm`, `vbaProject.bin`, macro sheets, macro content types), external links, more than 500 zip parts, an uncompressed size above 100 MB, a part above 5 MB compressing better than 1:200 (zip bomb) and invalid part names are refused; the first visible sheet that is not `Instructions` / `Allowed values` is read. `.csv` is UTF-8 (BOM tolerated), delimiter comma, semicolon or tab (sniffed), NUL bytes refused. Limits (settings, env overridable): 10 MB per file, 20,000 data rows, 60 columns. A text cell starting with a quote followed by `= + - @` has the guard quote removed, so an export re-imports unchanged. Headers match ignoring case, spaces, `_`, `-`, `/` and punctuation, by label, key or a legacy alias (`Prerequisite`, `Requirement ID`, `Steps`, `Execution Date`, `Sub Module` appended to the module path ...). A missing mandatory column is one whole-file 422 listing the columns (`error.fields`); unknown columns are warned and ignored; the derived columns of an export (actual result, execution status, defect ids, created by, executed by, execution date, comments/attachments, reporter, date reported, attachments, project name) are read and ignored.
4. **Preview, then commit.** `POST .../import/preview` (multipart `file` + `project_id`, 10/minute) parses and validates every row without writing anything and stores the parsed cells in the new table `import_sessions` (migration `c7d1e5a9b3f2`: tenant, user, kind, project, gzip JSON payload, counts, `expires_at` 30 minutes, status pending / queued / committed; RLS and grants like every tenant table; at most 40 MB compressed; expired rows are purged by the next preview of the tenant). The token is the unguessable session id and is scoped to tenant, user and kind (anything else is a 404). The response holds totals (rows, valid, warnings, errors, importable), per-row status with column-level messages, the first 200 normalised rows, up to 5,000 listed issues (`GET .../import/preview/{token}/rows?status=&offset=&limit=` pages and filters the whole stored result), file warnings, unknown / ignored columns, the modules that would be created and whether the commit will be asynchronous. Validation: mandatory fields (test case: module, title, priority, preconditions, test data, steps, expected result; defect: title, description, steps, expected, actual, environment, severity, priority), limits as in ADR-116 / ADR-118, enum values matched case-insensitively against the canonical value (or label) with a nearest-value suggestion, assignee by active user email (a defect also by a unique full name; a defect assignee must be a project member and the importer needs `defect.assign` / `defect.close`), module paths `Module/Submodule/Feature` matched case-insensitively and created when missing (only for rows that will be imported), linked test case ids must exist in the project (a scoped Tester only resolves the cases visible to them), labels as in ADR-118, defect status (code or the tenant's label) with the resolution rules of ADR-118 (Fixed defaults its resolution; any other initial status is allowed when the resolution fits; statuses other than New / Assigned need `defect.change_status` or `defect.close` and Closed / Rejected need the close permission; an approved or deprecated test case needs `testcase.approve` / `testcase.deprecate`). Display ids: kept when well formed (`TC_` / `BUG_` and six or more digits), not repeated in the file and not used anywhere in the tenant (soft-deleted included); a repeated id inside the file is an error, a malformed or already used id is a warning and a new id is assigned. A title that exists in the project or repeats inside the file is a warning (so an export re-imports with zero errors). Reporter is the importing user and the date is the import time.
5. **Commit.** `POST .../import/commit {token, mode}` (10/minute). The stored cells are validated again against the current database, then inserted in one transaction: modules, display ids (the counter first moved past the highest kept id, then one range reserved for the others), cases / versions / steps or defects / links (the first linked case is the primary FK, as in ADR-118), and audit events (one summary event `testcase.bulk_imported` / `defect.bulk_imported` plus one compact `*.created` event per record, appended to the hash chain in batches by `record_audit_events_bulk`). `all_or_nothing` (default) refuses the whole file when any row has an error (422, nothing written); `skip_invalid` imports the rest. Any failure rolls everything back, including counters, modules and audit rows, and leaves the token usable. The summary is `{created, skipped, failed, errors[], ids[] (row, id, display id), modules_created, batch_id}`. Above 2,000 importable rows the commit is queued on the existing job queue (job type `standard_import_commit`, session marked queued; the worker re-validates and inserts; a failure releases the token) and the response is `{async: true, job_id}`; `GET .../import/status/{job_id}` is readable by the creator only. Legacy endpoints (`/test-cases/import`, `/async`, `/mapped`, `/detect-columns`, `/defects/import`) are unchanged.
6. **Export.** Both exports (csv and xlsx) write the complete 22-field structure under the catalogue labels (the template headers), then the extras; `module` is the full path; steps use the single-cell format; `columns=` selects and orders, `ids=` selects rows (test cases gained it), the list filters apply, text starting `= + - @` is prefixed with a quote in csv and xlsx (test cases too), at most 50,000 rows (`X-Result-Truncated: true` when cut; the defect export used to answer 422 above the cap), the csv is sent chunked and the workbook has a bold, frozen header and sized columns. The old test case labels changed to the catalogue's (Prerequisite -> Preconditions, Requirement ID -> Requirement / User Story ID, Steps -> Test Steps, Defect ID -> Defect / Bug ID, Execution Date -> Last Execution Date, Comments / Attachments is one column) and `Sub Module` was folded into the Module path.
7. **UI.** One `ImportWizard` for both lists replaces the two CSV dialogs: choose file (template links, project picker on the cross-project defect list), review (summary chips, errors-only filter, row-wise problems with file row numbers, modules to create, error report CSV), confirm (all or nothing / skip invalid), result (progress for background jobs, counts, skipped rows, View imported). The legacy column-mapping import for files from TestRail, Zephyr or Jira stays reachable from the test case wizard (`MappedImportFlow`). Permission gated like the API.

**Deviations.** Expired previews are purged lazily (the next preview of the tenant), not by a scheduler. The 50,000-row export is built in memory (in chunks of 500 rows) and then sent chunked, not read lazily from the database, because the request session closes before a streamed body is sent. The test case Execution Status, Actual Result and the other derived columns are exported but never imported, so a re-import creates new cases rather than copying executions.

**Consequences.** Test case export column labels changed (tests and any consumer reading the old labels must follow). A file exported by the application previews with zero errors in the same project and imports as new records with new ids. Tests: `backend/tests/test_import_export_standard.py` (templates, readers and limits, preview without writes, token scoping and expiry, atomic commit with an injected failure, skip invalid, id preservation, the 5,000-row job, permission matrices, RLS, export parameters, round trips of both modules incl. steps, labels, links, enums), `frontend/src/lib/importExport.test.ts`, `frontend/e2e/import-export-standard.spec.ts`; migration `c7d1e5a9b3f2` up / down / up on a scratch database.


## ADR-121: Global navigation and top bar redesign, My Work, favourites and recent

**Context.** The sidebar was a flat list of single links held in a static `NAV_SECTIONS` array inside `AppShell`, with one badge (open defects), no way to group entries, and the same list copied into the phone More sheet and the command palette. The header carried nine controls (breadcrumb, inline search, a separate "Jump to" button, "+ New", an AI button, a theme icon, the bell and the account menu), and there was nowhere to collect what is waiting on the signed-in user or to return to records they use often. This is phase 2 of the QA-workspace plan: the global shell, with no change to any URL or permission.

**Decision.**

1. **Registry.** `lib/navRegistry.ts` is the single definition of the sidebar: top-level links and expandable groups (Dashboard, Projects, My Work, Test Management [Test Cases], Test Execution [Test Runs, Execution Console], Defects, Reports & Analytics [Reports, Compliance Reports], Admin). The desktop sidebar, the phone More sheet and the command palette read the same resolved list (`resolveRegistry`, `sheetSections`, `paletteEntries`), so a destination is added, renamed or hidden in one place. Visibility is not stored there: `lib/routeAccess.ts` decides, exactly as before, and the route gate uses the same function. A group with no visible entry disappears and a group with one visible entry renders as that plain link (Test Management holds only Test Cases today, so it shows as the Test Cases link; it grows into a group when Test Plans or Modules get top-level pages). Every entry needs a real page behind it: Calendar, Documents and Test Automation have none and are not listed (no placeholder pages), Releases and an organisation-level Team page do not exist and are not listed either (Admin keeps being the single entry to Users & Roles). The Dashboard keeps its label (the page heading, breadcrumb root and tests all say Dashboard) rather than becoming Home. Existing labels stay as leaf links with the same accessible names, and every existing route is unchanged.
2. **Groups.** A group heading is a non-link toggle button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`); the entries sit in a list that is hidden, not removed, when closed. Open or closed state is remembered per tenant and user in `localStorage` (`qtesthub:nav-groups:{tenant}:{user}`, everything open until closed, reads and writes in try / catch so blocked storage only costs persistence). The group holding the active route opens when you arrive on it (route change only, so closing it by hand sticks until the next navigation). The active entry carries `aria-current="page"` (one at a time; the new-tab Compliance link is never current) plus the existing tinted fill and accent bar. `/test-cases/:id` and `/test-run-cases/:id` now keep Test Cases and Execution Console highlighted. In the collapsed icon rail every link has an accessible name and a tooltip, and a group is an icon button (`aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`) opening a flyout (`role="group"`) of its links; Escape closes it and returns focus, any navigation or outside click closes it, and it is keyboard operable. The sidebar collapse button moved from the sidebar header to the top bar.
3. **Badges.** `useNavBadges` reads two real counts, each with its own query and a 60 s refresh: open defects (the existing status-counts query, ADR-118 definition) and **My Work** = the user's own pending run cases plus test cases waiting for their review, from the new `GET /my-work/summary` (the same queries as the My Work lists). A count that has not loaded reads 0 and hides the badge; nothing is estimated. Badge text is capped at 99+, the link's accessible name carries the count ("My Work, 3 pending"), and `useBadgeAnnouncement` writes a change after the first load into a visually hidden `aria-live="polite"` region (no focus change). Unread notifications stay on the bell. No count for "test cases awaiting my review" is shown separately: it is part of My Work.
4. **My Work** (`/my-work`, any signed-in user, `routes/mywork/MyWork.tsx`). Three tabs with live counts. Run cases (`GET /my-work/run-cases`): assigned to me, run not finished or deleted, status pending / in progress / failed / blocked, grouped In progress, Failed - retest, Blocked, Not run, each row linking to the Execution Console (`/test-run-cases/{id}?runId=`). Defects: the existing `GET /defects` filtered by `assignee_id` and by `reported_by` (open statuses, merged client-side, one row each, with why it is there). Reviews (`GET /my-work/reviews`): test cases in `ready_for_review` whose `reviewer_id` is me (the reviewer field of the review workflow). The only backend added is that one read-only router (`api/my_work.py`, `services/my_work_service.py`, `schemas/my_work.py`): no table, no migration, no permission code. Project visibility is the rule of the run list (Admin and Manager every project, others owned or member projects) and a scoped Tester (ADR-093) also only sees cases that scope allows, so the page can never show more than the lists it links to. Lists are capped at 200 with a note. Each tab has skeleton, empty and error (retry) states.
5. **Favourites and recent** (`lib/pinnedItems.ts`, `useFavourites`, `useRecents`, `components/RecordPin.tsx`). Items are keyed by type and id (`project`, `test_case`, `defect`, `test_run`, `requirement`) and store title and an app-relative URL. Storage is browser `localStorage` per tenant and user (`qtesthub:favourites:{tenant}:{user}`, `qtesthub:recents:{tenant}:{user}`), not the database: they are per-device conveniences, a record can be deleted or lose access after it was saved, and no migration or API was needed. It is a deliberate limit that they do not follow the user to another device; a `user_pins` table would be the next step if that is wanted. Parsing drops malformed entries and any URL that is not a same-app path, storage failures fall back to an in-memory copy for the page view, and a small external store (`useSyncExternalStore`, plus the `storage` event) updates the sidebar the moment a star is toggled. `RecordPin` sits in the detail page header of a project, test case, test run, defect and requirement: it renders the star (a labelled button, "Add to favourites" / "Remove from favourites", `aria-pressed`) and records the visit once the title is known; a renamed record updates its saved title. Favourites hold up to 30 entries, Recent the last 8 (a revisit moves the entry to the front). Both are collapsible sidebar sections (remembered like groups) and flyouts in the rail, appear in the More sheet and the empty command palette, and every entry has a remove button so a stale link (the record now 404s) can be cleared by hand. Nothing else deletes them.
6. **Top bar.** One 56 px row (`components/TopBar.tsx`). Left: the sidebar toggle (md and up) and the breadcrumb (current page only on phones). Centre: one wide search field (lg and up; an icon below) that opens the existing command palette and shows the Ctrl K (Cmd K on Apple devices) hint; the inline `GlobalSearch` dropdown and the separate "Jump to" button were removed because the palette already covers them. The palette now also finds projects and test runs (server-side `search` on the existing projects and runs lists) and lists the user's favourites and recent items. Right: "+ New" (the only filled control), the notification bell and one account menu. Appearance (the theme dialog) and Ask AI moved into the account menu, which also holds My Account and Sign out; the old header Appearance and AI buttons and the standalone theme icon are gone, and the More sheet keeps its own rows. The account button shows initials (and the name from 1440 px; its accessible name still starts with the visible name, WCAG 2.5.3). The User Guide stays in the sidebar footer, so there is no separate help icon. Controls are 36 px (44 px on phones), tooltips open below the bar, focus rings are the shared ring. All theme options work as before.
7. **Tests.** Vitest: `navRegistry.test.ts` (structure, role filtering, single-leaf collapse, active group and leaf, badges, palette and sheet consumers), `pinnedItems.test.ts` (keys, toggle, cap, recents order and dedupe, malformed and off-site entries, blocked storage, per-user isolation), `useFavourites.test.tsx` (hooks, cross-consumer updates, per-user lists, recording and removal), `myWork.test.ts`. Backend `tests/test_my_work.py` (sign-in required, only the caller's open assignments, blocked and finished runs, project visibility, reviewer-only reviews, summary equals list). Playwright `e2e/nav-workspace.spec.ts` (groups as toggle buttons with remembered state and auto-expand, keyboard, rail flyouts, favourites and recent end to end including per-user separation and removing a stale entry, My Work for a tester, a manager and an admin including loading and error states, the top bar at 1440 / 1280 / 1024 / 768, the phone layout and More sheet at 375, light and dark axe). Specs for the old header were updated where the UI intentionally changed: `themes`, `tier3-features` and `shell-phase-c` (Appearance and the search field moved), `phase-h` (Sign out).

**Deviations.** Home stayed Dashboard; Team Management, Releases, Calendar, Documents and Test Automation are not in the navigation (no real page or no decision to build one); Test Management shows as the Test Cases link until it has a second entry. The More sheet's Sign out colour moved from `--destructive` to `--status-serious` (the same meaning, enough contrast on the sheet surface).

**Consequences.** The next phase (project workspace) adds to `NAV_REGISTRY` and `useNavBadges` rather than to `AppShell`. `GlobalSearch.tsx` and the `NAV_SECTIONS` / `resolveNavItem` exports are gone. Favourites and recent are per browser; `localStorage` keys and the 30 / 8 caps are in `lib/pinnedItems.ts`. The sidebar `<nav>` is labelled "Workspace" (not "Primary", which names the phone tab bar in the specs).

## ADR-122: Project workspace: layout, switcher, header, tabs, module visibility and tab order

**Context.** A project was a dashboard page (`/projects/:id`) with a six-link tab strip (`ProjectTabs`) and one long "Details" page that held members, builds and releases, test suites, test plans and the edit form, 841 lines in one file. There was no way to change between projects without going back to the list, no per-project choice of tabs, and no way to edit a release. This is phase 3 of the QA-workspace plan.

**Decision.**

1. **One layout.** `routes/projects/ProjectChrome.tsx` holds `ProjectLayout`, a React Router layout route around every `/projects/:projectId/...` page, and `ProjectScoped`, a wrapper for the two tabs whose pages live outside the project path: `/defects?projectId=` and `/reporting?project=` (without that parameter they render exactly as before). Both render `ProjectHeader`, `ProjectNav` and the page. The project is read once with the shared `['projects', id]` query key (the pages use the same key), so there is no second request. A project that cannot be loaded (404 for a Tester outside it, 403) shows the error state instead of the page, as before.
2. **Header** (`ProjectHeader`). Name, status, priority, owner (`manager_name`), progress, favourite star (`RecordPin`), the switcher and, with `project.edit`, a Settings shortcut. The name is the page `<h1>` only on Overview; on every other tab it is a paragraph so each page keeps its own single `<h1>`. Progress is the approved share of the counts the project already carries (`approved_test_case_count / test_case_count`, "0 test cases" at zero); nothing is estimated. On a phone the header wraps into rows and every control is 44 px.
3. **Switcher** (`ProjectSwitcher`). A button that opens a searchable listbox (combobox pattern: `aria-activedescendant`, Arrow keys, Home / End, Enter, Escape returns focus). Data source: the existing `GET /projects` (`page_size` 100, sorted by name), read only while the list is open; no new endpoint. Recent projects (the `useRecents` store, ADR-121) come first, then A to Z. Choosing a project goes to `switchTarget`: the same tab in the target when that tab is available there (allowed for the user and not hidden by the target's own `nav_config`), else the target's Overview.
4. **Tabs** (`ProjectNav`, `lib/projectNav.ts`). Overview, Requirements, Test Cases, Test Runs, Defects, Reports are core (reorderable, never hidden); Planning (`/test-plans`), Releases, Team, Activity are optional and hideable; Settings (`/settings`) is shown only with `project.edit` and is never hideable. The label stays "Test Cases" and "Test Runs" (the sidebar and breadcrumb names), "Dashboard" became "Overview". Reports follows the route gate (`canAccessPath`, so a tenant that hides Reports from Testers also loses the tab). Documents, Automation and Calendar are not tabs: there is no real page behind them. The strip is a `<nav aria-label="Project sections">` of links with `aria-current="page"`; counts (test cases, runs, open defects from the project payload) are visual and `aria-hidden`, so a link's name stays its label.
