Tabs that do not fit (measured with a hidden copy and a `ResizeObserver`) move into a "More" menu (`DropdownMenu`); the active tab always keeps a place in the strip, so the page never scrolls sideways at 375 or 768 px.
5. **Moved, not rewritten.** `TeamMembersCard`, `BuildsReleasesCard`, `TestSuitesCard`, `TestPlansCard`, `ProjectDetailsCard` and `DeleteProjectButton` were cut out of `ProjectDetail.tsx` into `ProjectTeam`, `ProjectReleases`, `ProjectPlanning` and `ProjectSettings` with their queries, mutations and permission checks unchanged. `ProjectDetail` (`/projects/:id/details`, kept working) now only composes those same cards and the run-status / recent lists, which also open the Activity tab. All other URLs are unchanged; the new ones are `/test-plans`, `/releases`, `/team`, `/activity` and `/settings` under the project. `ProjectTabs.tsx` is removed.
6. **Module visibility and order.** `projects.nav_config` (nullable JSONB, migration `d2f4a6c8e0b1`) holds `{order, hidden}`; NULL means the defaults. `ProjectUpdateRequest` validates it against a fixed allowlist (`app/schemas/project.py`, mirrored in `lib/projectNav.ts`): unknown or repeated keys, a core tab or `settings` in `hidden` are a 422; a partial `order` is completed in default order when read. It is written by the existing `PATCH /projects/{id}` (`project.edit` plus the ADR-018 assignment rule, audited as `project.updated`, `expected_version` supported), so there is **no new permission code** and RLS is unchanged (a column on a tenant table). Settings > Project navigation edits it: Up / Down buttons per tab (no drag dependency, keyboard operable, a polite live region announces moves, focus stays on the pressed button), a Show checkbox for hideable tabs only, Save, and Reset to defaults (stores NULL, after a confirmation). Tenant-wide defaults are not part of this phase.
7. **Hidden means not shown, not blocked.** A hidden tab's URL still opens for anyone the API lets in; the chrome shows a note ("The Team tab is hidden from this project's navigation"), with a link to Settings only for people who can edit. Reason: `nav_config` is presentation, the API stays authoritative, and a bookmarked or shared link must not die when someone tidies the tabs. The switcher never targets a hidden tab.
8. **Releases.** `PATCH /releases/{id}` (`environment.manage`, the gate the other release writes use) edits `name`, `release_date`, `notes` and a new nullable `releases.status` (`Planned`, `In Progress`, `Released`, `Cancelled`; NULL for every existing release), sent fields only, null clears the optional ones. The release's project must be visible to the caller (`get_project`: a Tester outside it gets 404), another tenant's id is 404, and each change is audited as `release.updated` with a from / to per field. `POST` accepts `status` too. The Releases tab shows date and status and an Edit dialog.
9. **Breadcrumb and title.** The trail is Dashboard > Projects > project > tab (`Overview`, `Planning`, ...), also on `/defects?projectId=` (Defects) and `/reporting?project=` (Reports); browser titles read "Project Overview", "Project Team" and so on.
10. **Tests.** Backend `tests/test_project_nav_config.py` (round trip, allowlist 422s, tester / unassigned manager / assigned manager, audit and version, tenant isolation, release PATCH fields, validation, permission, audit and isolation). Vitest `projectNav.test.ts`, `ProjectNav.test.tsx`, `ProjectNavSettings.test.tsx`, `breadcrumbs.test.ts`. Playwright `e2e/project-workspace.spec.ts` (header, tabs, moved pages and /details, switcher, settings round trip, hidden-tab link, release edit, tester / manager / admin views, phone and tablet, light and dark axe). Specs that clicked the old Dashboard or Details tabs were updated (`project-dashboard`, journeys 1 to 4, `forms-phase-f`, `modules-phase-e`, `phase-h`, `reliability`, `tier3-features`).

**Deviations from the plan.** Settings, not "Details", carries the edit form; the Test Planning tab is labelled Planning; no tenant default for tab order; Automation and Documents are omitted (no real page). The project health signal on the header is the approved-case share, not a pass rate (project dashboards and run-case due dates are the next phase).

**Consequences.** New project tabs are added to `PROJECT_TABS` in `lib/projectNav.ts` and to the key lists in `app/schemas/project.py`. A `nav_config` key a later release removes is ignored when read and refused when written. The Defects and Reports tabs still leave `/projects/:id`, so their active state comes from the query string (`activeTabKey`).

## ADR-123: Project dashboards, run-case due dates and project report aggregates

**Context.** The project Overview (ADR-102, ADR-122) was a fixed, read-only page assembled from the ADR-099 widgets. It had no per-case execution figures (executed / passed / failed / blocked / not run), no module coverage, no tester comparison, and no way to say that work is late: nothing in the model carried a due date. This is phase 4 of the QA-workspace plan.

**Decision.**

1. **Organisation and project dashboards stay separate.** `/` (ADR-110, with the ADR-099 custom dashboards) aggregates every project the viewer can see and is behaviourally unchanged. `/projects/:id` is the project's own dashboard: it shows only that project, the project id comes from the URL and goes on every request (a required `project_id` query parameter; the API checks it against the viewer's scope, 403 / 404 outside it), and the UI says so ("Showing <project> only", a link "See all projects"). The `/dashboards` endpoints never list, open or edit a project layout, and a project layout never counts toward the 10-dashboards-per-user limit.
2. **Run-case due date.** `test_run_cases.due_date` (nullable `date`, migration `e3a5c7d9f1b4`). Set by `PATCH /test-run-cases/{id}/assign` (`due_date` independent of `assigned_to`: an unsent field is left alone, `null` clears), and by an optional `due_date` on run creation and "add cases" (applied to every case bundled). Validated to 2000-01-01 .. 2100-12-31, audited in the existing `testruncase.assigned` / `testrun.created` events, returned by every run-case read and by My Work (`due_date`, `overdue`). **Overdue** = `due_date` before today (UTC) and status `pending` or `in_progress` in an unfinished run; a blocked or failed case was acted on and is not overdue. The UI uses the same UTC "today" (`lib/dueDate.ts`) so a badge never disagrees with a count. No new permission: whoever may assign a run case may set its date.
3. **Aggregates** in `reporting_service.py`, each one SQL `GROUP BY` over one project's rows, wrapped in `@cached_report` (key = tenant, role, user and every argument, ADR-113), narrowed by the viewer's project scope and the ADR-093 Tester case scope, soft-deleted cases excluded:
   - `GET /reports/project-test-status`: total, executed, passed, failed, blocked, skipped, not run, execution % and pass rate, from the **latest executed run case per case** (the ADR-116 derived status; `_latest_exec_select` gained a `project_id` filter and a `lean` mode that skips the per-row step-executor lookup). `executed + not_run = total`, and every figure equals the Test Cases list total for the matching `execution_status` (the list gained `Executed` = any result, so the KPI link lands on a list that really applies it).
   - `GET /reports/module-coverage`: per module total / executed / passed / failed / blocked / not run / coverage %, equal to the `module_id`-filtered list.
   - `GET /reports/tester-performance`: per person the cases whose latest result they recorded (the list's `executed_by` filter), pass rate and defects raised (the defect list's `reported_by`).
   - `GET /reports/overdue-run-cases` and `GET /reports/upcoming-milestones` (dated releases that are Planned / In Progress / unset, plus each open run's earliest future due date).
   Everything else reuses existing endpoints: `/dashboard/summary` (open and critical defects with the 30-day baseline), execution trend, release readiness, workload, requirement coverage, activity stream, the defect list (recent defects) and `/my-work/run-cases` (which gained an optional `project_id`).
   `project-test-status`, `module-coverage`, `overdue-run-cases` and `upcoming-milestones` stay open to a Tester when Reports are hidden from Testers, like `/dashboard/*` (the Overview must render and they count only what the Tester may already list); `tester-performance`, a comparison between people, honours that switch (403).
4. **Layouts** reuse `dashboards` / `dashboard_widgets`: nullable `dashboards.project_id` (FK, ON DELETE CASCADE), the owner/default CHECK widened to a third shape (owner NULL + `project_id` = the project default), partial unique indexes for one layout per (project, user) and one default per project. `GET / PUT / DELETE /projects/{id}/dashboard` (own layout; any viewer of the project) and `PUT / DELETE /projects/{id}/dashboard/default` (`project.edit` through the project-role override plus the ADR-018 assignment rule, so an Admin or an assigned Manager; audited `dashboard.project_default_set` / `_cleared`). Resolution: own layout, else the project default, else the built-in order. A layout stores only widget type, size and order (a hidden widget is absent; an empty layout is valid); the catalogue is fixed server-side (14 types, at most 20, no repeats, 422 otherwise) and sizes stay S / M / L (ADR-099: no free drag or resize, no new dependency). No new permission code.
5. **UI.** The Overview is a KPI strip (8 compact, clickable tiles; a previous-period trend only on Total test cases and Open defects, the two figures with real history) plus the widgets, each with a title, a **View** link to the records, loading / empty / error states (a 403 says "Not available for your role"), and for charts the ChartPanel table view and CSV menu. **Customise** (a dialog) shows or hides widgets and orders them with Up / Down buttons and checkboxes (focus stays on the pressed button, a polite live region announces moves); Save keeps a personal layout, Reset drops it, and people who can edit the project also get Save / Remove project default. The header progress now reads "n% executed (x/y) . z% pass rate" from the same aggregate (one shared request), falling back to the approved share if the viewer may not read it. The run page has a Due column (editable by managers, Overdue badge otherwise) and an optional due date when creating a run; My Work and the Execution Console show due dates and Overdue badges; `/execution?projectId=&overdue=1` narrows the Console queue (the widget's View link).
6. **Links.** Every link lands on a list that applies its filter: KPIs and the status distribution use `executionStatus` (Pass / Fail / Blocked / Not Run / Executed) on the project's Test Cases, module rows `moduleId`, tester rows `executedBy` and `reportedBy`, open defects the Open tab statuses with `severity` where relevant, overdue the filtered Execution Console, milestones the Releases tab or the run.
7. **Performance.** No new table. Indexes (migration `e3a5c7d9f1b4`, CONCURRENTLY inside `autocommit_block`): `ix_test_run_cases_tenant_due` and `ix_releases_tenant_project_date`; the status aggregates reuse the ADR-113 indexes. Measured on a load-test tenant with one 50,000-case project: see "Measured" below.
8. **Tests.** Backend `tests/test_project_dashboard.py` (17: each aggregate against the list endpoints and raw SQL for a seeded project with a soft-deleted executed case and a second project, case-scoped Tester, hidden Reports, due-date semantics and audit, My Work, layout round trip, validation, precedence, permissions, scope 404 / 403, org separation). Vitest `projectDashboard.test.ts`, `dueDate.test.ts`, `CustomisePanel.test.tsx`. Playwright `e2e/project-overview-dashboard.spec.ts` (KPIs against the API and the lists, overdue / upcoming, due-date editing, customise round trip with per-user layouts and the project default, case-scoped Tester, org separation, phone and tablet, light and dark axe); `project-dashboard` and `project-workspace` were updated for the new Overview and header.

**Measured.** One throwaway tenant with a single project of 50,000 test cases, 125,000 run cases (15,582 overdue), 25,000 defects and 2,500 requirements (`seed_loadtest.py --projects 1 --scale 5`, dev stack on a slow machine): cold (cache miss) `project-test-status` 219-288 ms, `module-coverage` 208-247 ms, `tester-performance` 269-314 ms, `overdue-run-cases` 65-72 ms, `upcoming-milestones` 65-69 ms; cache hits about 20 ms. No new index beyond the two above was needed. The existing requirements coverage endpoint behind the traceability widget returns every requirement (368 KB, about 280 ms at 2,500 requirements) and is the next candidate for a summary-only variant. The tenant was dumped and removed afterwards.

**Deviations from the plan.** Sizes are not editable (fixed per widget). The Tester-scoped Overdue count follows the case scope, while the Execution Console queue it links to is built from the run payloads and does not apply that scope, so for a case-scoped Tester the two can differ. "Skipped" (run case status `ignored`) is part of Executed but has no list filter of its own. The Defects KPI trend compares with 30 days ago using the existing summary baseline; the other six tiles have no history and show none.

**Consequences.** A new widget needs an entry in `PROJECT_WIDGET_CATALOGUE` (`app/schemas/dashboards.py`), `WIDGET_META` / `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` (`lib/projectDashboard.ts`) and `PROJECT_WIDGETS` (`routes/projects/overview/widgets.tsx`). A widget type removed later is ignored when read. Phase 5 (views) can reuse the due date for a calendar and `/execution?projectId=` for a board.


## ADR-124: Views: List / Board / Calendar over the existing records

**Context.** Defects, test cases and test runs were reachable only as tables. Teams that triage by status, or plan a week of execution, had no way to see work laid out by state or by date, although every field needed was already stored (status, run timestamps, the ADR-123 run-case due date, release dates). This is phase 5 of the QA-workspace plan. Zoho Projects was inspiration only: there is no Gantt (no task dependencies exist), no timeline, no mock data and no placeholder view; each view is a different layout of the same endpoints.

**Decision.**

1. **View switcher and persistence.** One control, `ViewSwitcher` (a pressed-button group like the "Assigned to me" switch), in the shared `ListToolbar` (`view` slot; on a phone it sits above the filter bar). Modes: Defects List / Board; Test cases (project list) List / Board; Test runs (project list and all-projects overview) List / Board / Calendar. The mode is `?view=board|calendar` (List is the default and is not written to the URL), so a view is shareable and survives a refresh. It is also remembered per user and per list in `localStorage` (`qth:view-mode:<user>:<list>`; every storage call is wrapped, a blocked store only loses the memory). URL wins over memory, memory over List; a value the list does not offer is ignored. `lib/viewMode.ts` is the one implementation.
2. **Saved views carry the mode.** `view` (`list | board | calendar`) is one more allowlisted key inside the existing `saved_views.filters` JSON (defects, test_cases, test_runs, requirements, projects): no new column, so every view saved earlier is still valid and, having no `view`, leaves the current mode alone when applied. `test_runs` also allows `overdue` (`true|false`, the calendar's "Overdue only"); the Phase 4 filters `moduleId` and `executedBy` were already allowed for test cases (`moduleId` for defects). The list-key CHECK was widened (migration `f5b7d9a1c3e5`) and `requirements` / `projects` accept `search`, `projects` also `status`. Those two lists have the keys on the server but no Saved views menu yet: they keep their filters in local component state rather than the URL.
3. **Boards.** `BoardView` is presentational (columns, cards, counts, Load more, announcements); `lib/board.ts` (`useBoardColumns`) is the data side: one paged query per column using the list endpoint filtered to that column's status, so the column total is the server's, a column loads 20 cards and **Load more** fetches the next page, and nothing loads a whole table. Cards show display id, title, severity / priority, assignee, an overdue-aware due date where the record has one (none of these three records carries its own due date: run-case due dates are on the calendar), and one muted line.
   - **Defects by status.** Columns are the nine workflow statuses (Open and Verified, the legacy ones, appear only when a defect is in them, or when the Status filter names them). A card may move only to the statuses the workflow table allows from its status (`transitionMenu`, which also applies the close permission). A drop uses the same rules as **Change status**: Retest opens the existing **Start retest** dialog (it creates the Retest run), a move that needs a resolution or the verification tick opens the existing **Change status** dialog with the target preselected, and every other move calls `PATCH /defects/{id}/status`. A refusal (409 / 422 / 403) is shown, the card stays in its column, and nothing is moved optimistically: the card is dimmed with "Moving to X" until the server answers, then the columns refetch.
   - **Test runs by status.** In progress / Paused / Finished. Status is derived from the run's timestamps, so a move is one of the run's own actions: pause, resume, finish, reopen (`/test-runs/{id}/pause|resume|finish|reopen`). Allowed to whoever may use them on the run page (`testrun.finish` and the manager tier).
   - **Test cases by execution status** (Pass / Fail / Blocked / Skipped / Not Run) is **read-only**: the status is derived from each case's latest executed run case (ADR-116) and never stored, so there is nothing to drag. The board says so and links to the runs. The review status (Draft ... Deprecated) is not a column set: it changes through submit / approve / request-changes / deprecate, each with its own permission and dialog, so it stays a filter tab and a badge on the card. Deprecated cases are included on the board.
4. **Keyboard, touch and screen readers.** Every movable card has a **Move to...** menu (menu pattern, Escape returns focus), which is also the touch path; native HTML5 drag and drop is the pointer path (no dependency). A polite live region announces every outcome ("DEF-12 moved to Assigned", "DEF-12 was not moved. ...") and a visible alert shows a refusal. People without the move permission get neither a drag handle nor the menu (the cards are still links). Columns scroll horizontally, with snap on a phone only (82% width cards; snapping while a drag is in progress moves the pointer's target). The Move to menu is positioned with `position: fixed` from the button (`DropdownMenu` gained a `fixed` option; it opens upward near the bottom of the window and closes on scroll or resize) because a column's scroll container would clip an absolutely positioned menu. A card dropped on a column the workflow does not allow is refused with the reason (every other column accepts the drop so the refusal can be shown).
5. **Calendar.** A month view for test runs: runs on the day they started and the day they finished, run cases by due date (one entry per day and open run: "2 due: Smoke", red when overdue, never colour alone: an icon and a text label per kind), and releases by release date; each entry links to its record. It is a roving-focus grid (arrow keys by day and week, PageUp / PageDown by month, Home / End within the week) with a day agenda under the grid; below 768px it is an agenda list of the month's days instead of a 7-column grid. The month (`?month=2026-11`) and "Overdue only" (`?overdue=true`) are in the URL. There is no week view and no Gantt. A test run has no planned start or end date in the model, so the calendar uses the actual ones (creation and finish); adding planned dates would be a model change.
6. **Backend.** `GET /calendar?from=&to=&project_id=` (`calendar_service`): three single queries (runs started or finished in the range, run cases grouped by (day, open run) with open / total counts, releases dated in the range), at most 62 days (422 otherwise; the UI asks for the month's six-week grid), each list capped (300 / 500 / 300) with `truncated`. Any signed-in user, no new permission: it shows what the run and release lists already show: project scope as the run list (Admin / Manager every project; others owned or member projects; an unassigned `project_id` is 403, unknown 404), the ADR-093 case scope narrows the due counts, soft-deleted runs are excluded by the ORM, "overdue" is the ADR-123 UTC rule. Not cached: it is per-user date-range data, not an aggregate. `GET /test-runs` gained `run_type` so a board column honours the page's Type filter. Boards use the existing list endpoints (`GET /defects`, `/projects/{id}/test-cases`, `/test-runs`, each with its status filter and `total`), so no grouped-count endpoint was added.
7. **Permissions.** A board move needs exactly what the equivalent action needs: defects `defect.change_status` (or create / close), Closed and Reopened the close permission; runs `testrun.finish` plus the manager tier (as the run page buttons); the server re-checks every call. A Tester sees on a board, and on the calendar, only what the lists already show.
8. **Quality.** Light and dark through the existing tokens; boards and calendar have loading skeletons, empty, per-column error with retry, and `aria-busy` while a move is pending; motion is limited to an opacity transition that honours reduced motion; tap targets are at least 24px (44px on a phone for the Move to button, Load more, month buttons and the Overdue toggle).
9. **Tests.** Backend `tests/test_calendar_views.py` (the calendar for a range, grouping and overdue flag, finished and soft-deleted runs left out, project scoping, Tester project and case scope, validation, auth; saved views: new keys, the mode key, bad values, old views). Vitest `BoardView.test.tsx` (counts, Move to menu, refusal keeps the card, drag and drop, read-only, Load more, column error), `CalendarView.test.tsx`, `viewMode.test.tsx`, `calendarGrid.test.ts`. Playwright `e2e/views.spec.ts` (switcher persistence, defect board move and refused move, Move to menu with live region, test case board read-only, run calendar with real due dates, phone agenda, light / dark axe).

**Measured.** No large project exists on the dev stack now (the Phase 4 load-test tenant was removed; the largest project holds 32 cases), so the board and calendar reads were not timed against 50,000 cases and that figure is not claimed. What bounds them by construction: a board column reads one page of 20 through the existing list endpoints (their ADR-113 / ADR-116 indexes), the calendar is three LIMITed queries over a 42-day window using `ix_test_run_cases_tenant_due` and `ix_releases_tenant_project_date` (ADR-123) and `test_runs(tenant_id, project_id, created_at)`, and nothing returns more than 20 cards per column or 1,100 calendar rows. The one unmeasured risk is the per-column `execution_status` list on a very large project (the derived-status join of ADR-116).

**Deviations from the plan.** No calendar week view and no Gantt. The Test Repository (cross-project) list and the Requirements and Projects lists have no board. A board is not offered for the approval status of a test case (see 3). Requirements and Projects accept saved views on the server, but their lists do not show the menu yet.

**Consequences.** A new board is a `useBoardColumns` call plus a card mapper and, if cards move, `targets` and an `onMove` that uses the real endpoint. A new dated record on the calendar is one more list in `calendar_service` and one mapper in `RunsCalendar`. Phase 6 (workspace settings) can hide the Board / Calendar modes per tenant by filtering the `modes` array each list passes to `ViewSwitcher`.

## ADR-125: Workspace settings: tenant defaults for the sidebar, project tabs and list views

**Context.** The sidebar (ADR-121), the project tab strip (ADR-122, per project) and the board / calendar views (ADR-124) were the same for every organisation. An admin could not tidy the menu for their teams, and a project could only be tailored one by one. This is phase 6 of the QA-workspace plan. Zoho Projects was inspiration only: nothing here is a mock, every switch changes what people really see, and no permission is relaxed.

**Decision.**

1. **One small document on the tenant.** `tenants.workspace_config` (nullable JSONB, migration `a6c8e0b2d4f7`) holds `{sidebar: {order, hidden}, project_tabs: {order, hidden}, view_modes: {list_key: [modes]}}`. NULL means the built-in defaults, so every existing tenant is unchanged, and saving the defaults stores NULL again (Reset leaves no residue). The tenants table already carries tenant RLS, so no policy was added. The document is validated by `schemas/workspace.py` (unknown top-level or nested keys, unknown or repeated entries, hidden core entries and a list view that is switched off are all 422). A stored value that no longer validates reads as the defaults, so a stale document can never break the shell for everyone.
2. **Sidebar.** The admin orders and shows or hides the top-level entries of `lib/navRegistry.ts` (Dashboard, Projects, My Work, Test Management, Test Execution, Defects, Reports & Analytics, Admin). Dashboard, Projects, My Work and Admin are core: they can be moved but never hidden, and the API refuses a document that hides them. `applyNavConfig` is applied before the existing access filter, so the desktop sidebar, the phone More sheet and the command palette (which also drops the Report builder when Reports is hidden) read the same result, and the phone bottom tab bar drops a tab whose entry is hidden and re-flows its columns. Calendar, Documents and Test Automation are not entries: they have no real page, so they stay absent by default and are not offered as switches (approved decision). Favourites and recents stay per user and per device.
3. **Project tabs.** The same up / down and show / hide editor as Project Settings, over the Phase 3 tab list and the Phase 3 server rule (`NavConfig`: core tabs and Settings cannot be hidden). It is the default for every project whose own `nav_config` is NULL: `effectiveNavConfig(project.nav_config, workspace.project_tabs)` is used by the tab strip, the project switcher's "same tab in the other project" rule and Project Settings > Navigation (which shows the effective tabs; its "Reset to defaults" still stores NULL, which now means "follow the workspace default"). A project's own configuration always wins.
4. **List views.** `view_modes` narrows what each list passes to `ViewSwitcher`: defects (list, board), test cases (list, board), test runs (list, board, calendar; the project list and the all-projects overview share the key). `useListModes` filters the list's built-in modes; List is always kept and the order is the built-in one. A list left with only List shows no switch at all, and a hand-typed `?view=calendar` for a mode the tenant has turned off falls back to the list (the existing `resolveViewMode` already does this for any mode that is not allowed).
5. **API.** `GET /settings/workspace` for every signed-in user (the shell needs it), `PUT /settings/workspace` replaces the whole document and needs `tenant.settings.manage` (no new permission code; project-level tab edits keep `project.edit`). A change writes one hash-chained audit entry `tenant.workspace_updated` with the before and after documents; saving an unchanged document writes nothing.
6. **Cosmetic only.** Hiding removes an item from a menu. Its URL still opens (the project chrome keeps its "this tab is hidden" note), `routeAccess.ts` still filters on top, and the API enforces every permission exactly as before. The page is `/admin/workspace`, listed under Admin > Workspace and reachable only with `tenant.settings.manage` (`ROUTE_ACCESS` permission rule), so a Manager or Tester has no menu entry and gets the no-access page by URL, and a 403 from the API.
7. **Frontend.** `api/workspace.ts`, `lib/workspaceConfig.ts` (`useWorkspaceConfig`: one cached query, 5 minute stale time, falls back to the built-in defaults while loading, on error and when nothing is stored), `routes/admin/WorkspaceSettings.tsx` (loading skeleton, error state with retry, one Save and one Reset with confirmation, keyboard operable up / down buttons with focus kept on the pressed button and a polite live region, 44px targets on a phone).
8. **Tests.** Backend `tests/test_workspace_config.py` (defaults, round trip, reset stores NULL, 22 invalid documents, audit before / after and no entry for an unchanged save, stale document, auth, tenant isolation) and `tests/test_rbac_matrix.py` (Admin, Manager assigned to the project, Manager not assigned, Tester, and a read-only custom role walked through the same operations against a table of expected codes: workspace GET / PUT, project `nav_config`, project dashboard default and personal layouts, project scope of the dashboard and its aggregates, org dashboards never listing project layouts, saved views privacy, defect board moves, close and reopen, run finish, calendar project and case scope, cross-tenant isolation). Vitest `workspaceConfig.test.ts`, `WorkspaceSettings.test.tsx`, `MobileNav.workspace.test.tsx`. Playwright `e2e/workspace-settings.spec.ts` (an admin changes the defaults and a Manager and a Tester of the same tenant see the sidebar, tabs and list switches change; core entries cannot be hidden in the UI or API; a project's own tabs win; reset restores everything; a non-admin has no menu entry, no page and no write access; axe in light and dark at 1440, 1280, 768 and 375).

**Deviations from the plan.** The default project dashboard widget set per tenant was not built: the Phase 4 default is stored per project (a `dashboards` row with `project_id`), so a tenant default would need either a second storage path or a new fallback tier in `project_dashboard_service`; it is not a cheap reuse and is left out rather than faked. No new permission codes (decision 7). Hiding applies to top-level sidebar entries only; the leaves of a group (for example Compliance Reports) follow their group.

**Consequences.** A new sidebar entry appears in the Workspace page by being added to `NAV_REGISTRY` (and to `NAV_ENTRY_KEYS` in `schemas/workspace.py`, which the API uses to validate); a new project tab is picked up from `PROJECT_TABS` and `ALL_NAV_KEYS` as before; a new list with a board or calendar adds a key to `VIEW_MODE_LISTS` and `LIST_VIEW_MODES` and calls `useListModes`. A real tenant-wide "module off" (blocking the API) is deliberately not part of this: it would be a second authorisation layer.


## ADR-126: Workspace redesign: final audit, known limits and what was not built

**Context.** Phases 2 to 6 (ADR-121 to ADR-125) replaced the shell, added My Work, the project workspace and tabs, the configurable project Overview, the Board and Calendar views and the admin Workspace settings. This is phase 7 of the QA-workspace plan: prove the whole redesign is correct, accessible, responsive, fast enough and documented, and close what the audit finds. Every figure below is from the dev stack in Docker on a slow, memory-starved Windows machine, run one test at a time.

**Audit result.** The plan's completion criteria were walked against the running app (rebuilt `api` and `web` images): the registry sidebar with groups, badges, favourites and recent; the My Work page; the top bar and command palette; the project workspace (header, switcher, tabs, module visibility and order); the project Overview dashboard with its Customise panel and its aggregates; run-case due dates; List / Board / Calendar views; the tenant Workspace settings; and the RBAC matrix across Admin, assigned Manager, unassigned Manager, Tester and a custom role. All pass; the evidence is the named backend tests (`test_workspace_config`, `test_rbac_matrix`, `test_project_dashboard`, `test_calendar`, `test_project_nav_config`, ...) and Playwright specs (`nav-workspace`, `project-workspace`, `project-overview-dashboard`, `views`, `workspace-settings`, and the extended `a11y-sweep` and `mobile-screens`).

**Decisions and fixes made in this phase.**

1. **`/projects/:id/details` redirects to the project Overview** (`LegacyProjectDetailsRedirect`). The page was a re-assembly of cards that now live in the Overview, Team, Planning, Releases, Activity and Settings tabs and nothing linked to it; the Overview is the one tab every member can open (Settings needs `project.edit`). `ProjectDetail.tsx` was deleted and its breadcrumb and page-title entries removed. Specs that opened `/details` now open `/activity` (which carries the recent-lists drill-down) or assert the redirect.
2. **Test Management label.** The sidebar shows a one-leaf group as its leaf (ADR-121), so Admin > Workspace listed "Test Management" while the menu said "Test Cases". The behaviour was kept (the group grows when Planning or Modules get top-level pages) and the Workspace row now says so. The user guide says the same.
3. **Requirements coverage summary.** `GET /projects/{id}/requirements/coverage?summary=true` returns the three totals with `requirements: []`, computed by two COUNT queries; the Overview's Requirement traceability widget uses it. At 2,500 requirements the full report is 368 KB and 230 to 500 ms; the summary is 0.2 KB and about 80 to 140 ms. The default (full) response is unchanged for the Requirements list. Test: `test_coverage_summary_matches_the_full_report_without_the_rows` (totals equal the full report, a requirement with two cases counts once, soft-deleted cases drop out of both).
4. **`useApplyDefaultView`** (a default saved view must not overwrite a filter the user already has) is proven for filters by `viewsHelpers.test.tsx`: the default is applied once when nothing is set; not applied when the URL has filters; not applied when a filter is set while the views are still loading; nothing happens when no view is default. Still open: a sort chosen before the views arrive is not tracked (`urlFilters.active` has no sort), so a default view that carries a sort can replace it on a first visit. The patch was not reverted.
5. **Accessibility fixes found by the new sweeps.** Project Settings for a person without `project.edit` rendered the inline no-access block with no `<h1>` (axe `page-has-heading-one`, Tester); it now renders the page variant. Three off-scale `rounded` classes (sidebar pin button, search shortcut key, calendar day link) failed the existing design-scale test and became `rounded-md`. Two specs were corrected, neither a product defect: `brd-phase2` counted a deleted record's title in the sidebar Recent list (Recent keeps a link until its x is pressed, by design) and `nav-workspace` measured contrast during a colour transition (it now waits with `settle`).
6. **Tests added to the existing suites.** `a11y-sweep.spec.ts`: My Work, the five moved project tabs, three boards, the calendar and `/admin/workspace` join the route sweep (Admin, Manager, Tester), and a new test scans ten workspace pages at 1440 light, 1280 dark, 768 light, 375 dark and 375 light for every role: axe at every impact level and no horizontal overflow, and it reports (does not fail) targets under 24 px and, on a phone, under 44 px. `mobile-screens.spec.ts`: the same pages at 390 px with an overflow and 24 px target check, the agenda calendar and 44 px Workspace controls. `e2e/a11y.ts` gained `settle` and `smallTargets`.

**Measured** (one throwaway tenant, one project of 50,000 test cases, 125,000 run cases, 25,000 defects, 2,500 requirements; deleted afterwards, name-guarded). Cold is the first call per user; warm is repeats inside the 20 s report cache. Milliseconds, cold admin / manager / tester, then warm p50, after `ANALYZE`:

| endpoint | cold | warm |
|---|---|---|
| project-test-status | 163 / 204 / 247 | 29 |
| module-coverage | 212 / 193 / 201 | 23 |
| tester-performance | 422 / 445 / 462 | 27 |
| overdue-run-cases, upcoming-milestones | 29 to 41 | 22 to 27 |
| dashboard summary, execution-trend | 171 to 207 | 23 to 30 |
| release-readiness | 707 / 289 / 423 | 24 |
| requirements coverage, full / summary | 227 to 465 / 129 to 143 | 369 / 110 |
| project dashboard layout, workspace GET | 33 to 132 | 36 to 110 |
| calendar (project / all projects) | 77 to 185 | 83 / 100 |
| nav badges: defect status-counts / my-work summary | 58 to 68 / 156 to 164 | 63 / 148 |
| board columns, defects and runs | 52 to 651 | 50 to 106 |
| **board columns, test cases by `execution_status`** | **534 to 2545** | **754 to 968** |
| traceability matrix (the page, not the widget) | 2019 to 3794 | 162 |

A first pass on freshly loaded tables (before `ANALYZE`) was slower on the report endpoints (dashboard summary up to 1.2 s, release-readiness up to 1.1 s). Over about 500 ms cold: the four test-case board columns, the traceability matrix and, for the first user only, release-readiness. The test-case board is the risk ADR-124 named: each column is the list endpoint with the ADR-116 derived-status join, uncached, and a board loads four columns at once. It is acceptable on a normal project and slow at 50,000 cases; a grouped-count endpoint with `cached_report` is the next step and was not built here because it needs its own contract and tests.

**Import timing.** `test_bulk_import_2000_rows_under_2_5_seconds` still fails (4.0 s alone, 7.4 s under load; the threshold is unchanged). Profile: the import is already batched (`insertmanyvalues`, 16 statements for 2,000 cases). About 3 of the 4 seconds is the three multi-row INSERTs inside PostgreSQL (2,000 cases about 1.05 s with 15 indexes, 9 foreign keys and the display-id trigger on `test_cases`; 2,000 versions about 1.0 s; the steps about 1.0 s) plus the driver's statement splitting; the Python work (validation, display ids, step parsing) is under 0.2 s. There is no per-row query left to remove, so no fix was made. The 3.05 s measured in ADR-115 grew with the indexes added since (ADR-113, ADR-116, ADR-123); dropping or deferring an index would trade away list speed and is left to a decision.

**Known limits.**

- WCAG 2.2 target size (2.5.8) is reported, not enforced: breadcrumb links (20 px tall), the "View" links on dashboard widgets (16 px) and legend links on the Overview are under 24 px on desktop. axe at WCAG 2.1 AA is clean at every impact level. On a phone the primary controls meet 44 px; secondary text links in boards and lists are 28 px.
- Favourites and recent are per browser; a deleted record stays in Recent until its x is pressed.
- The Dashboard summary can be up to 20 s stale on the other API worker after a write (ADR-113). `dashboard-project-filter.spec.ts` depends on that: it fails with a 20 s TTL and two workers and passes with `REPORT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS=0`.
- Hiding a menu entry or tab is cosmetic (ADR-125); the API enforces every permission as before.

**Deliberately not built.** Gantt and timeline (no task dependencies exist), project templates, the Calendar, Documents and Test Automation modules (no backing data; no placeholder pages), an organisation-level Team page, and tenant default dashboard widgets (the default project dashboard is stored per project; a tenant tier needs a second storage path or a new fallback in `project_dashboard_service`). Overdue and upcoming items exist only because run cases carry a due date (ADR-123); test cases and defects still have none.

**Consequences.** The audit leaves no open product defect. Open items, by value: a cached grouped-count endpoint for the test-case board; sort handling in `useApplyDefaultView`; larger targets for widget "View" links and breadcrumbs if WCAG 2.2 AA becomes the gate; pruning Recent when a record is deleted; the import timing decision above.

## ADR-127: Project > Task > Sub-task hierarchy, and moving records inside their project

**Context.** Everything a project owns (requirements, test cases, runs, defects) hung directly off the project, joined only by the requirement <-> test case link. There was no way to say "these requirements, test cases and defects belong to one piece of work", no unit of planning below a release, and no way to ask how that piece of work was going. Records also opened at top-level URLs (`/defects/:id`) outside the project they belong to.

**Decision.**

1. **A `Task` sits between Project and its records, and is optional.** A project has many tasks; a task can have sub-tasks (up to five levels, `MAX_TASK_DEPTH = 4`); a requirement and a defect carry a nullable `task_id`; a test case is linked to tasks many-to-many (`task_test_case_links`) because one case verifies several tasks. Nothing existing was changed or backfilled: a record with no task is simply unassigned, so a tenant adopts tasks task by task.
2. **Hierarchy rules, enforced by PostgreSQL where they can be and by the service where they need context.** Database: a sub-task is in its parent's project (composite foreign key `(parent_task_id, project_id) -> tasks(id, project_id)`); `depth` is 0 for a top-level task and exactly parent + 1 below it (CHECK plus a trigger); a task is not its own parent; the trigger also walks the new parent's ancestors, so a task can never be moved under itself or one of its own sub-tasks (the depth check alone is not enough: making a root the child of its own child satisfies `depth = parent + 1` row by row); a requirement or defect can only point at a task of its own project (composite foreign keys); `TASK_000001` display ids are immutable. Service: a task cannot be completed while a sub-task is open; a done task cannot take an open sub-task and a sub-task of a done task cannot be reopened (reopen the parent first); a sub-task's due date cannot be later than its parent's, nor a parent's earlier than a child's; moving a task moves its subtree and rewrites every descendant's depth parent-first (the row trigger validates each row against its parent); a parent with sub-tasks is deleted only with `cascade=true`, deletion is soft, and requirements and defects that pointed at the deleted tasks lose the link but are kept.
3. **Statuses are configurable per tenant, with a fixed meaning.** `task_statuses` holds the tenant's code, label, colour and order; each belongs to a fixed `category` (`todo`, `in_progress`, `done`) that the code relies on for completion, overdue and roll-ups. One default per tenant and it is a `todo`; at least one `todo` and one `done` always exist; a status that tasks use (soft-deleted included) cannot be deleted or change category. Priority reuses the existing configurable field options (`task_priority`).
4. **Three permissions.** `task.manage` (Admin, Manager): create, edit, move, delete, link test cases. `task.transition` (Admin, Manager, Tester): change a status; a person without `task.manage` may change only the status of a task assigned to them. `task_status.manage` (Admin): the statuses. Reading follows project access (a Tester must be assigned to the project). Freeze windows can block them: `task` is a valid blocked action prefix (derived from the permission codes; `task_status` is a separate action).
5. **The task page and roll-ups.** `/projects/:pid/tasks/:tid` shows the task, its path, five roll-up numbers (progress, requirement coverage, pass rate, open defects, test runs) and a tab each for requirements, test cases, defects, runs, discussion (comments and attachments, new owner type `task`) and activity. The numbers cover the task and, unless switched off, every sub-task. A task's test cases are those linked to it directly plus those linked to its requirements; a result is a case's latest executed run case in a live run. Health ("At risk", "Needs attention", "On track") is derived from those numbers only. The Tasks tab also has a Hierarchy, a Flat list and a Board (one column per status; a move calls the status endpoint and shows its refusal).
6. **Records open inside their project.** Requirements, test cases, test runs and defects (detail, edit, new) now live under `/projects/:projectId/...`, so the project header and tab strip stay around them. The old top-level URLs remain as redirects that look the record up and replace the URL, so bookmarks, pins, recents and notification links keep working; a record opened under the wrong project moves to its own. The execution console and the cross-project lists (`/defects`, `/test-runs`, `/test-repository`, `/execution`) did not move. `lib/entityRoutes.ts` is the one place that builds the URLs.
7. **Reporting.** A "Task progress" widget on the project Overview (`GET /projects/{id}/tasks/summary`); requirement coverage and the traceability matrix (JSON, CSV, Excel) take `task_id` and `include_sub_tasks` and gain a Task column; tasks are found by title or `TASK_` id in search and the command palette; the report builder has a Tasks source and defects a Task field; requirement and defect lists filter by task.

**Phases.** 1 data model (migration `b7d1f3a5c9e2`), 2 backend (`c8e2a4b6d0f1` permissions), 3 project integration and status admin, 4 task workspace (`d1f4a6c8e0b3` comments and attachments), 5 routing, 6 reporting, 7 this audit.

**Audit result and fixes.** The data model, API, permissions, UI and routes were walked against the running behaviour and the seams a new project-level entity touches. Four defects were found and fixed in this phase, each with a test (`tests/test_tasks_audit_api.py`):

- **Deleting a project that has tasks (or requirements) was an unhandled foreign-key error.** `delete_project` checked test cases, runs and defects only. It now answers 409 for tasks (soft-deleted ones too: they still reference the project) and for requirements (a gap that predates tasks).
- **Deleting a release that tasks reference was the same error.** Tasks planned for the release now lose it (`release_id` set to NULL); the tasks stay.
- **The tenant data export left the tasks out.** `task_statuses`, `tasks` and `task_test_case_links` are in the export, soft-deleted tasks included.
- **The task tree capped at 2,000 tasks without saying so.** `total` is now the real number, and the Tasks tab and the task picker say when the list is partial.

Also done: the report-cache table list now includes the three task tables; the migrations were exercised up, down and up again on a database with data (`tests/test_task_migration.py`: seeded statuses, priorities and permissions; old requirement and defect rows survive the downgrade; task comments, counters and permissions are removed by it and the old constraints return); the role matrix is tested for Admin, Manager, a Tester (own tasks only) and a custom role holding only `task.manage`.

**Measured** (`tests/test_tasks_performance.py`, in-process, one project of 13,100 tasks in four levels, 3,000 requirements, 2,000 defects and 2,000 linked test cases; best of three, milliseconds, a slow shared machine):

| endpoint | ms |
|---|---|
| tree, capped at 2,000 tasks | 1,430 |
| list page of 25 / search / category / overdue | 130 / 165 / 120 / 85 |
| project summary (widget) | 90 |
| roll-up of a 131-task subtree | 150 |
| effective test cases of that subtree | 120 |
| requirements, defects, coverage, matrix for a task and its sub-tasks | 100 to 150 |
| report builder over all 13,100 tasks | 125 |
| move a 131-task subtree under another task | 300 |

Every figure but the tree is under 350 ms. The tree is the slowest because it serialises 2,000 tasks with their direct counts; it feeds the hierarchy view and the pickers, which is why the cap exists.

**Known limits.**

- A project with more than 2,000 tasks shows the first 2,000 in the hierarchy and the picker (it says so); search and the filtered list reach the rest.
- Roll-up counts of a task's own row are direct only (sub-tasks, requirements, defects, test cases); the task page's numbers include the subtree.
- Deleting a task is soft, so a project that ever had a task cannot be deleted (history is kept).
- A task's test runs are the runs that contain one of its cases; there is no direct task <-> run link.
- Defect and test case CSV import/export did not gain a Task column: both are defined by the 22-field standard (ADR-115), and widening it touches templates, validators and the field catalogue. The report builder exports tasks and the defect-to-task link instead.
- The execution console, My Work and a few dialogs still build old top-level record URLs; they work through the redirect with one extra request.
- The sidebar highlights Projects, not Test Cases or Test Runs, on record pages, because they now live in a project.
- Not run in a browser in this phase: the Playwright specs `record-routes.spec.ts` and `task-reports.spec.ts` are written and typed but were not executed.

**Deliberately not built.** Task dependencies, Gantt and timeline, task templates, time tracking, and a tenant-level task list across projects.

**Consequences.** A new project-level entity has to be added in five places that are easy to miss: the project delete check, the tenant export, the report-cache table list, the owner-type constraints if it takes comments or attachments, and `lib/entityRoutes.ts` if it has a page. Open items, by value: the Task column in the defect and test case import/export; a lighter payload for the tree; moving the last top-level record links to `entityRoutes`.

## ADR-128: Requirements live in tasks (no separate Requirements section)

**Context.** After ADR-127 a task can hold requirements, test cases, defects and documents, so the project-level Requirements tab and list duplicated what the task page already shows. Removing the requirement *entity* would have lost its immutable versions, the requirement-to-test-case traceability, the compliance coverage figures and the AI requirement features, and would have merged two different things: a task is a unit of work, a requirement is a statement of what the product must do, and one task usually has several.

**Decision.**

1. **The section goes; the entity stays.** The Requirements tab, its list page and its "New requirement" dialog are removed. Requirements are created, listed and managed on their task (the task page's Requirements tab), and a requirement page is part of the Tasks tab. Versions, links to test cases, the Traceability Matrix, compliance coverage, the AI features, search, saved report counts and the tenant export are unchanged. `/projects/:id/requirements` redirects to the Tasks tab; requirement pages stay at `/projects/:id/requirements/:rid`.
2. **Every requirement has a task.** `requirements.task_id` is NOT NULL. A requirement created without one, detached (`task_id: null`), or whose task is deleted moves to the project's **Unassigned requirements** task (`tasks.is_requirements_home`), created on first need under an advisory lock, at most one per project (partial unique index). The migration files every existing task-less requirement there first, numbering the new tasks from the tenant's `TASK_` counter.
3. **That task is protected.** It cannot be deleted, moved or given sub-tasks (service rules; the picker leaves it out as a parent), and it is marked in the Tasks list and on its page. It can be edited like any task, so assignee, dates and status work.
4. **Navigation.** `requirements` is removed from the project tab keys; a saved project or tenant layout that still names it is accepted and the key dropped, so nothing needs re-saving. The Tasks list gets *Traceability matrix* and *New Requirement* buttons; the top bar's "New requirement" opens that dialog (`?action=new-requirement`); the matrix page belongs to Reports; a requirement's breadcrumb points at Tasks.

**Not done, and why.** A project-wide list of every requirement was not kept: the task tree, the matrix, search and the report builder cover it. Removing the entity (option C in the plan) was rejected for the loss described above. The downgrade makes the column nullable again and drops the flag but leaves the default tasks, which are ordinary tasks.

**Consequences.** A project that had loose requirements shows one extra task and counts it in task totals and roll-ups. Anything that creates a requirement must go through `requirement_service.create_requirement` (it is the only creator); any new code that deletes tasks must move their requirements to the default task as `delete_task` does.

## ADR-129: Tasks in My Work, and a Task column in import and export

**Context.** Tasks have assignees and due dates (ADR-127), but My Work did not show them, and neither CSV nor Excel carried the link between a record and its task.

**Decision.** `GET /my-work/tasks` lists the caller's tasks that are not in a `done` category, using the same project visibility as the rest of My Work, which matches the rule for opening a task; the summary counts and the sidebar badge include them. The standard import and export gain one last, optional `Task` column that holds task display ids, which are unambiguous where titles are not. A defect takes one id (`defects.task_id`); a test case takes several (`task_test_case_links`). Import never does more than the task page allows: an id outside the project is a row error, and linking a test case needs `task.manage`, including a project role override. Each imported link is recorded in the task's history.

**Not done.** The legacy 11/13-column import and the column-mapping import do not read the column. A file moved to another project reports its task ids as errors rather than dropping them silently. Conditional access rules on `task.manage` are evaluated on the task routes only, not per imported row.

## ADR-130: A flat sidebar

**Context.** The sidebar had three groups, one of which held a single page. The Execution Console and the compliance report had entries of their own although each belongs to another page, and the compliance entry opened a new tab.

**Decision.** The sidebar is one list: Dashboard, My Work, Projects, Test Cases, Test Runs, Defects, Reports, Admin. The group code is removed. The Execution Console is opened from Test Runs and from My Work, and counts as part of Test Runs for highlighting; the compliance report is the Compliance tab of Reports, and a person who may only export it still gets a Reports link that leads there. Both stay in the command palette. A saved workspace layout (ADR-125) that names a former group id is translated on read. This matters because a document that fails validation is ignored whole, so without it removing an id would silently reset a tenant's sidebar, tabs and view modes.

**Consequences.** Before an entry's id is removed or renamed in future, it needs the same translation (`RENAMED_NAV_ENTRY_KEYS` in `schemas/workspace.py`). The Execution Console must keep a link from Test Runs: it has no sidebar entry.

## ADR-131: Eight project tabs

**Context.** The project strip had eleven tabs. Releases and Planning are two halves of one subject, Settings duplicated the gear already in the header, and Activity is occasional reading.

**Decision.** The tabs are Overview, Tasks, Test Cases, Test Runs, Defects, Reports, Planning and Team. Releases and builds are the second section of Planning; `/projects/:id/releases` redirects there. Settings and Activity are icon links in the project header, which mark themselves when current. Activity is there for everyone and no longer depends on an Overview widget or a tab layout. Team stays a tab: the page is open to people who may not edit the project, whereas Settings is not.

The merged tab has a new key, `planning`, so that old and new layouts cannot be confused: an old layout hiding only `test-plans` keeps Planning visible, because Releases was visible, while a new one hiding `planning` hides it. `NavConfig` translates old keys before validating. This is required, not a courtesy: a project's `nav_config` is validated every time the project is read, so refusing a stored key would make the project, and the project list, fail to load.

**Consequences.** Activity can no longer be hidden. A later removal or rename of a tab key needs the same translation (`MERGED_NAV_KEYS` / `REMOVED_NAV_KEYS` in `schemas/project.py`).

## ADR-132: Two Test Cases pages that behave alike

**Context.** The all-projects page and a project's Test Cases tab share a title but ordered their shared columns differently, saved filters in different places, and only the tab could export.

**Decision.** The pages stay separate; merging them would risk the features only one has (bulk priority and assignee there, the board and the module filters here). The shared columns now come in the same order. The all-projects page uses saved views, stored per user on the server under their own list key, `test_repository`: its filters differ from the tab's, and a default view on one must not apply to the other. Migration `a7c9e1f3b5d8` adds the key to the table's check. Export is offered when one project is chosen, with the tab's four choices; the export API is per project.

Filters saved by the old control lived only in the browser. Each user's first visit copies them into their views. The originals are not removed, so a failed or partial copy loses nothing, a second person on the same browser gets their own copy, and names already copied are remembered so that deleting a view does not bring it back.

**Not done.** Exporting across all projects at once needs a new endpoint.

## ADR-133: Less above the first record, and the phone bar

**Context.** On a 390x844 screen the project header, tabs and page introduction pushed every row of the task and defect lists off the first screen. The phone bar offered Test Cases and Runs but neither My Work nor Projects.

**Decision.** A record's page shows a compact project header (name, switcher, Activity, Settings) at every width; on phones every project page but the Overview does. Lists leave out their back link and description on phones, Export shares a row with the other actions, and Tasks drops the column picker that cards ignore. The bar is Dashboard, My Work (with its pending count), Projects, Defects and More; Test Cases and Test Runs are in More. A tab row that overflows is faded with an arrow on that side, and the selected tab is scrolled into view, again when late counts widen the tabs.

**Consequences.** On a phone the Execution Console is More, Test Runs, then its button. The first record's title is visible without scrolling on the task, defect and test case lists; its whole card is not always.
